# @skillcdn/console

## 0.5.2

### Patch Changes

- [`f282ee9`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f282ee98d050601a95e8e451ade457ca47d98da0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - What a day of an agent account on a machine showed of the runner's own words:
  
  - **A direct message at Slack outside any thread is said as a message of the conversation.** The `slack` wake source said every message was "in the thread" of its own moment, and the agent answered each in a thread. It names a thread now only where the message was written in one.
  - **A product whose command is not there is said so.** A session's record said `The product could not be started: spawn claude ENOENT`; it says that the command was not found where the runner looks for programs, and what to do. The runner's own lines say it when it starts, where the product does not answer for its version.
  - **Claude Code 2.1.329** is among the builds the `claude` driver was run against, so an account on it is no longer marked as on a version the driver was not tried with.

- [`d80838b`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d80838b3b4bb966c85ec4bcf5f98b9c3a3cf4bf9) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin's job that keeps failing is told to a reader who may not read why in words that say so. The problem sent everyone to `plugin.failed` among the plugin's events; that event is written in the plugin's board project, and a member the project is closed to reads none of it. Such a reader is now told that the reason is in that project, by its key, and that a member of it or an administrator reads it. The reason itself is still said nowhere but in the event. A plugin whose records are behind its code, an upgrade having failed, is told the same way.

- [`d9cfa06`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d9cfa06804d750316e1c8d4e3435abd219b04d8d) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The button of a file field stands in the middle of the field's height. A project's form draws the field for its image as tall as the fields beside it, and the button the browser gives it and the words beside the button stood against the field's top edge, with all of the room that was left under them: a browser lays the two out on a line of its own at the top of the field, whatever the field's `display` says, in Firefox, Chrome and Safari alike. The room is now the field's own padding, half above and half below.

- [`f282ee9`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f282ee98d050601a95e8e451ade457ca47d98da0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: setting a machine up for an agent account gives the machine its least, and leaves the rest to the agent ([ADR-0140](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0140-a-machine-is-given-its-least-and-the-agent-does-the-rest-itself.md)). What changes for whoever runs or builds on it:
  
  - **The invitation's line** now reads "Set this machine up for an agent account. Install what is missing yourself, without asking: ... Ask me only for a sign-in or an administrator's password." A language pack of one's own writes `agents.page.settings.machine.made.line` anew.
  - **`console agent setup` looks at the machine before anyone signs in**: git (on a Mac the Command Line Tools first), the command of the agent's product (`claude`, then `codex`, or the one `--driver` names), and for Claude Code Google Chrome, which its browser tool works in (`--no-browser` for an agent that signs in at no page). Everything missing is said at once, with what installs it, and the command ends there with exit 1. A script that ran the setup on a machine without these now stops before the sign-in.
  - **The command prints the part of whoever prepares the machine, and no more.** `setupGuideText` prints the sections whose `who` is `operator`, and the section `machine` is one of them now; the guide's page and `--json` carry it whole. The steps of the guide changed: nothing offers to type what an account signs in with, and no password is to be written in a chat.
  - **A setup ends by handing over**: whether the agent's first session began, where to watch it, its first task, and that the machine's part ends there.
  - **`runner.json` takes `"browser": "chrome"`**, which starts Claude Code with its browser tool (`--chrome`); a new desk names it where the browser was found, and names the driver of the product found.
  - **A desk the setup writes or clones is a repository whose git reads `git.token` from the account's store** for the workspace's git host, in place of the machine's own credential helpers. A desk that is there is left as it is.
  - **`console secret <name> --set` and `console agent secret` say when a value does not look like what its name holds** (`slack.app_token`, and `git.token` at GitHub or GitLab), or holds a space. The value is kept all the same, and never said.
  
  The skill `running-an-agent` is rewritten for this, and is shorter.

- [`7e1fdba`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7e1fdba2099dac8fca0d33d5e893125a66df736c) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - Breaking: `formatInstant` writes a moment without its year in the year it is, and its hour as the language writes one, never in two digits: "Nov 16, 1:33 PM" where it wrote "Nov 16, 2026, 01:33 PM". It takes the moment it is read at as a third argument, for a test. `Time` writes the same at every precision, and the feed writes its hours as the language does where it wrote a clock of twenty-four. A line of an agent's conversation is read against the line before it: it says its hour alone on the same day, and its day where the day changes (ADR-0135).

- [`7e1fdba`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7e1fdba2099dac8fca0d33d5e893125a66df736c) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - Breaking: a page is at most a hundred rem wide, `--sc-width-page`. In a window wider than about 1780 pixels `--sc-gutter` grows to take what is over, so the header, the tabs and the page keep one edge and a plugin's frame is bounded with its page. A page that wants the whole window, the board, the calendar, a goal's board or a page that says it is wide, keeps the gutter a wide window had, now `--sc-gutter-wide` (ADR-0136).

- [`a753315`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a753315a739b889c817c20b7f1aae643d3cecf08) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin's page no longer takes the mouse wheel from the console's page. Its frame is as tall as the page says, and the console's page is what scrolls, the sub-tabs with the page; but the height a page said was its body's `scrollHeight`, which a browser rounds to the nearest pixel, so a page 516.4 pixels tall got a frame of 516 and its document a fraction of a pixel to scroll. Firefox scrolls that fraction under the wheel and holds the wheel to it for a moment: over a plugin's page a turn of the wheel often moved nothing, or the page alone under its sub-tabs. A page's frame is now embedded with `scrolling="no"` and never scrolls itself, also while its height catches up with a page that grew, unless it stands at the most a frame grows to, 20,000 pixels; this holds for every plugin once the console is updated. `connect()` of `@skillcdn/console/plugin/web` now says the body's height as drawn, rounded up, so that a plugin built with this version has a frame never shorter than its page.

- [`9b0f9a6`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/9b0f9a6bcbfc05078e1dbc20907cb8e0b45d0de5) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The image gives a plugin's database role its password in the form the server keeps one. With `storage:sql`, the statement that makes or changes the role carried the password the console draws at every boot, and a PostgreSQL that logs its statements (`log_statement` of `ddl` or more) wrote it into the server's log. The statement now carries a SCRAM-SHA-256 verifier, a salt and two keys which alone sign nobody in, and the password stays in the console's memory. A server whose log held such a statement holds a password that was replaced at the console's next boot. A server set up to refuse a password given in that form refuses the role, and `storage:sql` says so as it says any refusal of the role.
  
  A statement of `ctx.sql` is one statement, and its connection goes back to the pool reset. A text that held several statements and no parameter once ran every one of them, a `begin` that left the pool's connection inside a transaction among them, and then failed with words about a list; the server now takes such a text for no statement and runs nothing of it (`plugin.invalid_args`). After a statement, and after a transaction, the connection is reset (`DISCARD ALL`): a timeout the plugin set for its session is the role's own again, and an advisory lock or a temporary table taken for the session is let go, where the pool handed the same connection to the next call with all of it still held. A plugin that relied on something of a session outlasting its statement, which the contract never promised, no longer finds it.

- [`572d35a`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/572d35a78d84df19dc6a46c225f91ae55a1f3ec7) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - Breaking: the progress bar is drawn in the colours of a chart's series, what is done in the green `--sc-series-6` and what is at work in the blue `--sc-series-1`, where it wore the tone for success and the accent, and a gap of two pixels parts the two, as a share's parts are parted. `ProgressBar` no longer renders the element of a part of nothing. A bar of your own drawn on `sc-progress-bar` reads best in the series' colours too (ADR-0138).

- [`b013ed8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b013ed8f2b39c53100195897c74fa7e6b317c885) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A project's mark may be an icon of the console's pack. A project gains `glyph`, the name of one, which the mark shows on the project's colour in the symbol's place; the project's own image still stands over both.
  
  - The project's form chooses it with the pack's picker, beside the mark's letters, the colour and the image, and `ProjectMark` draws it from `project.glyph`.
  - `console project new` and `console project edit` take `--glyph <name>`, `none` to take it away; a name that is none of the pack's is answered with the pack's names before anything is sent. `console project <key>` says `glyph:` on the line of the mark, between the symbol and the image.
  - Over REST, `glyph` is optional on what makes and changes a project and takes a name of the console's own pack alone; in an answer it is the name or `null`, and a console older than the field leaves it out. `projectGlyphOf` reads it: a name the reader's pack lacks is none, and the mark is its symbol.
  - A deployment's `migrate` role adds the column (`0064_project_glyph`).

- [`f282ee9`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f282ee98d050601a95e8e451ade457ca47d98da0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A runner takes its `runner.json` as it changes, and says how its wake sources stand ([ADR-0144](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0144-a-runner-takes-its-runner-json-as-it-changes-and-says-how-its-wake-sources-stand.md)). An agent that added a wake source to its `runner.json` was not woken by it until someone restarted the service on its machine. The runner now looks at the file with every heartbeat: one that reads and changed is taken between two turns, in the same process and with the same conversation; one that does not read changes nothing, and the agent is told what is wrong with it.
  
  Each wake source says whether it is `connected`, `waiting` or `failing`, and why. The heartbeat carries that as `sources`, the console keeps it, and the account's page and `console agent <handle>` show it. A console before this version ignores the field, and a runner before it says none.
  
  For whoever builds on the package: `createRunner` takes `reconfigured`, and `run()` may answer `"changed"`; a `WakeHost` has `stands(state, why?)`, which a wake source of one's own may call and a host of one's own, in a test say, has to have; `restAgentRunnerSchema` reads an optional `sources`.

- [`f282ee9`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f282ee98d050601a95e8e451ade457ca47d98da0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account wakes itself, now or at a moment to come ([ADR-0145](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0145-an-agent-account-wakes-itself-now-or-at-a-moment-to-come.md)). `console agent wake me "<words>"`, on the command signed in as the account, raises a wake the account's runner takes as any other: for whoever prepared its machine, or signed something in there, to say so. `--in <minutes>` or `--at <HH:MM>` keeps it for later instead, one under a name (`--key`), which `console agent signals me` lists and `--cancel` takes away: for an agent that has something to look at again. A wake and nothing else, thirty within an hour and twenty kept at a time; what tells, stops or starts an agent stays its supervisors'.
  
  The client has `raiseOwnWake`, `ownWakes` and `cancelOwnWake`, over `POST`, `GET` and `DELETE /api/v1/agents/me/wakes`; a console before this version has no such route, and the command says so.

- [`f282ee9`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f282ee98d050601a95e8e451ade457ca47d98da0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: an agent account's profiles say its name and its role, and nothing of what it is ([ADR-0143](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0143-an-agent-accounts-profiles-say-its-name-and-its-role.md)). The first task no longer tells an agent to "say in your profile that you are an agent", and the desk's template no longer says "I am an AI agent, and I say so when it matters": it says that the agent works as any member does, that its profiles and what it signs carry its name and its role, and that it never says it is a person. An organization that wants its agents named as such says so in its rules.
  
  The template's `AGENTS.md` is also shorter: what a session is told when it starts, and how a sub-agent is briefed, are said once.
  
  A desk written before keeps the old sentences, since `console agent setup --refresh` writes over no file that is there. To bring one in line, the account changes two places itself: in `README.md`, "I am an AI agent, a member of my own there" becomes "I am a member of my own there"; in `AGENTS.md`, under "Who I am", the last sentence becomes "I work as any member does: my profiles and what I sign carry my name and my role, and nothing else. I never say I am a person." And it changes its profiles where it set them.

- [`938541c`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/938541c7342f5d381eb51c6744a5e6d5fa257a10) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - An agent's conversation keeps to the width of a narrow window. When the agent said a thing stood under its words a picture's width too far to the right, and a badge in a step's row, a sub-agent's name, never gave way, so the conversation scrolled sideways on a phone. The time now takes the room that is left, and the badge is cut short once the call's line has given all of its own.

- [`6b2388b`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/6b2388b18fa587af90c2ef0c778c789896497604) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: an agent account's page reads what the agent said as Markdown, and what its sub-agents did under the call that started each (ADR-0141). What the agent said stood on the page as the characters it was written in, a table as rows of bars and code between its fences; it is now rendered to elements by the component that renders what is written on the board: headings, lists, tables, code, links that open elsewhere, raw HTML as text and never as HTML. A picture named there is not fetched: it is shown as its words, a link to where it is. Two lines with no empty line between them are one paragraph from now on, as Markdown has it. What the agent was told, a thought, a tool's call and what it answered stay text that keeps its spaces and its lines.
  
  A sub-agent's events were shown where they arrived, each with a label, so that three at work made one line of their steps. The call that starts a sub-agent is now its row: what it is called, whether it runs, is done, failed or was stopped with its turn, how many tools it called and how long it ran; opened, what it was started with, what it did, a line each, and what it answered. While a turn is under way the conversation's foot says how many sub-agents are at work, with each under it by its name, what it is at, its count and its time, which the page counts by itself. All of it is read off the record, and a session recorded before reads the same way.
  
  The runner writes one fact more: the `tool_call` that starts a sub-agent carries `starts`, what the call calls the sub-agent, so that a page knows of it before its first event arrives. The `claude` driver says it; the other two say nothing of sub-agents, as before.
  
  `Markdown` takes `picturesAsLinks`, for a text nobody looked at before it is shown: a picture named in it is left a link and nothing is fetched. A language pack of one's own gains `agents.page.live.agent` (`label`, `calls`, `states`, `steps`) and `agents.page.live.working.agents`.

- [`c0438e8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/c0438e84f9f1b7bc1ea35a59e3b93479ea177846) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: arranging the front page starts from the page closed up, and a page kept with gaps can be closed (ADR-0139). The page of someone who has arranged nothing draws a tile in a box that need not be a size it comes in, the lead half the page wide; when they pressed "Edit layout" each tile took the cells nearest that box, so on twelve columns bands of empty columns opened beside the lead and at the page's edge, and moving one tile kept them. Arranging now starts with the tiles in the page's order at the first room from the top, a widget at the size the page told it or at a larger one of its `sizes` where the rows close with that, never a smaller, and the projects icons as they were, their own row ending short. Where the page already stands every tile on whole cells, a lead alone with the projects beside it, arranging starts on those very cells as before.
  
  While arranging, "Close the gaps" closes the width at hand by the same rule, for a page that was kept with gaps before; it is offered where it would move something, a project made a card stays one, and no widget is made smaller. A language pack of one's own gains the word `home.tidy`.
  
  On a grid of six columns the page nobody arranged stands the widgets that come two cells wide beside a lead that leaves two columns, one under the other, then the projects, where the projects alone left most of those cells empty and the widgets stood under the lead three cells wide.

- [`7e1fdba`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7e1fdba2099dac8fca0d33d5e893125a66df736c) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - Breaking: the Korean pack writes a particle in the form its word takes where it wrote both forms, in the feed, a task's history, a decision's lines and the plugins' settings; a Latin word is read as a loan word is, which is a guess. A small label is cased by the new `--sc-label-case`, `uppercase`, and `none` on a page in Korean, so a Korean label no longer has its Latin words alone in capitals. A task has one word in Korean. `projects.members.since` of a language pack is the words before and after the moment, `{ before, after }`, where it was one word before it: a console with a pack of its own gives the two (ADR-0137).

- [`d91531b`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d91531b63857fede6582bc58fa23b7eff590450c) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - Lines break where they read best, on every page and in a plugin's frame, which reads the same stylesheet. A sentence leaves no word alone on its last line (`text-wrap: pretty` on the page), and what is a title, a task's in a row, a decision's question, a callout's title, the words of an empty state, breaks into lines of about one length (`text-wrap: balance`), as the headings already did. A date or a time in a `time` element is never broken in two. What is typed, a field or a text area, and what is written out as it was, a `pre`, is left as it is. A browser that knows neither value wraps as before.

- [`2fac663`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/2fac663509f19b068ff88498cca1e9e2d1d1bf96) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console serve --records <dir>` says whether the directory is there as it found it when it began. It asked once it was listening, so a directory made in that moment was said to have been there, where the line should have said that nothing is kept yet and that what comes to be kept is served with no restart. What is served is as before.

- [`b026408`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b02640832eac264dd469510494f8b8401328c008) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The console has an icon pack ([ADR-0142](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0142-the-console-has-one-icon-pack-drawn-on-one-grid-and-used-by-name.md)): some eighty glyphs drawn on one grid of twenty with one stroke, for the console's pages, a page of your own, a plugin's and a prototype, so that an icon is taken by its name and not chosen or drawn anew wherever one is needed.
  
  - `Icon` draws a glyph by its `name` in the colour of the text around it, at `xs`, `sm`, `md` or `lg`, hidden from a screen reader beside words and a named picture with a `label`. `ICON_NAMES`, `IconName` and `isIconName` say what the pack holds, `ICON_GROUP_LIST` its groups.
  - `IconPicker` chooses one, or none: a field that shows the choice and opens the pack under it, found by a glyph's name or by the words a language pack gives it (`icons.words`, with `findIcons`), and worked whole with the keys.
  - `@skillcdn/console/icons` is the pack on its own, with no dependency, for a plugin's plain bundle: `iconElement(name, { size, label })` makes the element, and `GLYPHS` is the data.
  - The look is a part, `icon` (`sc-icon*`), in `console.css` and in the catalogue; a custom console's own stylesheet that drew `.sc-menu-caret`, the dots of a card's menu, a column's fold or a session's device by their element inside the page's class now meets the pack's `sc-icon` there instead.
  - The pages draw the glyphs they drew before from the pack. A column's fold is a shade lighter than it was; the others are as they were.
  
  The language packs gain `icons` (the picker's words, the groups' names and each glyph's search words) and `projects.form.icon`: a pack of your own needs them.

- [`dd11fe3`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/dd11fe3db6527338c04aea5be910850c5fded4a9) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - Breaking: the colours of a series run blue, coral, raspberry, teal, violet, green. The coral is second, where the teal was, and the teal fourth, so that a chart of two series, the commonest, puts the blue beside the coral: the pair that stands furthest apart to full colour vision and to a reader who does not tell red from green. The coral is calmer (`#e45b63`, where it was `#fa4655`), and the raspberry a step deeper (`#bf1b7f`, where it was `#bb2b76`) to stay apart from it; the six pass the same checks as before. A plugin that drew with `--sc-series-2`, `--sc-series-3` or `--sc-series-4` draws its series in the new colours.

- [`b8ae548`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b8ae5483af5197d849e021ba06b19296c9faf992) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The line an agent account's invitation gives to say to the agent's product is in the language of whoever reads the page. On an account's page a new invitation showed `Run: console agent setup ... (before it: npm i -g @skillcdn/console), then do what it prints` in English under Korean words; the command and the install stay as written, and the words between them now come from the language pack, as `agents.page.settings.machine.made.line`: a pack of one's own adds that word. `console agent invite` and the guide a code opens say the line in English as before, as everything the command prints is.

- [`56fb1d1`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/56fb1d137a29b468b67de6728e7906e6b943f439) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The command is built on SkillCDN 0.5.3. A long skill read with `console skill` still comes in parts, and a part after the first now begins under the skill's name, its source and its license alone: the description and the lists of its files and of what it refers to are printed once, with the first part, where every part repeated them before. Where a part ends inside a file of the skill the place is marked `(Continues in the next part.)`. The line a part ends with, and the console's own words under it that the next command is run now, are as they were. A console that reads its pack from a directory finds a name joined by capitals by its words too, as SkillCDN 0.5.3 reads one.
  
  The image is built on the release of its HTTP framework that holds a request's body to its limit however it is sent: a body sent in very many small pieces was kept piece by piece, and a request within the limit could take far more memory than its size. Every route of the console that reads a body is behind that limit, a plugin's own endpoints among them, so a console is updated to this image rather than left on the one before.

- [`7e1fdba`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7e1fdba2099dac8fca0d33d5e893125a66df736c) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - Breaking: a pass over the pages, so that they read as one.
  
  - A goal's page is headed by the goal, as a task's is by the task: `GoalView` takes `allHref`, the way back over the title, and puts the ways to edit the goal and to put its finished tasks away beside the title, where they stood among its facts. The part `detail` gains `sc-task-heading` and `sc-task-parent`, which were the task page's own.
  - The categories are a view of the settings, their rows on shared columns, with quiet buttons. A project's settings are edited from the head of their pane, as the workspace's are.
  - The feed's page is titled as its tab is named and stands on a pane. The archive keeps the board's tab lit. The page of a person's own work has its title first, and the agents that need them under it.
  - A form over a list is as wide as the list, `sc-panel-form-lead`; the form that connects an agent keeps a form's width. What is said under a field keeps to the width prose is read at.
  - On an agent's card the reason it stands as it does and since when are two lines, and a title is one line, cut short. On a person's card the title is not said twice. A session's badge stands beside the first line of when. Disconnecting an agent is a quiet button.
  - A waiting decision asks for a word to add once an answer is chosen. A member's card says since when in the language's own order. A new project's image has the room a file's name needs, a goal's name on the months the room to be read, and a card's title fills its lines, as it did.
  - The shell is drawn once the workspace's lists are known, so that the plugins' tabs do not arrive under the pointer.

- [`4860099`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/486009949be59d6cafb5a7addea68b8848ff8ffe) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console agent signals` says of each signal whether it is delivered. The account's line said how many were, `3 of 4 delivered`, and the record did not say which one waits. `GET /api/v1/agents/<handle>/signals` and `GET /api/v1/agents/me/signals` now answer `delivered` beside the page (`restSignalsSchema`, optional), the number of the last signal delivered with every one before it, as the account's runner last said, and the command writes `delivered` or `waiting` on each line. A console before this one leaves the number out, and the command then says nothing of it.

- [`f282ee9`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f282ee98d050601a95e8e451ade457ca47d98da0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A runner holds its machine awake while it runs. An agent account's machine with nobody at its screen went to sleep when idle, and the agent was not heard from. `console runner` now starts the system's own helper for as long as its process is there: `caffeinate` on macOS, the system's request to stay awake held by a small script on Windows, `systemd-inhibit` on Linux. It needs no administrator and changes no setting of the machine. The runner's own lines say whether it holds ("the machine is kept from sleeping while the runner runs"), and `console agent setup` ends by saying so, asking the system on macOS, with what is left to whoever keeps the machine: a sleep someone asks for, a lid that is closed, and logging the OS account in after a restart. An account set up before gets this with the update of the command, with nothing to do. The helper for Windows was run on Windows 11; those for macOS and Linux were not run on a machine yet.

- [`519b831`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/519b831abc49554a7b95ee5efed9c88dbe46d0a6) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The skills the package carries: `working-the-board` 1.40 says where a pack skill's own questions are asked when its work is on the board, its inputs in chat where the person is in the conversation and written on the task, what it has a person approve as a decision, both on the board where nobody is in chat; and that a file which only carried words to a command is removed once the command took it. `keeping-a-desk` 1.4 says how to read the two sections of the package's README a desk's page needs without reading the file whole. `running-an-agent` 1.19 uses the Korean pack's word for a task in its Korean description.

- [`eeb1881`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/eeb188117c3f29b4c037fcc83aac717c5870ef54) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Two sentences of the Korean pack take the particle their word asks for. When the Korean word for a task became one that ends in a consonant, two sentences that are written out whole kept the particle of the word before, which ended in a vowel: what a plugin's `board:tasks` capability opens, on its settings page, and the line of a board at a glance with no task at work. They take the object particle and the subject particle of a word that ends in a consonant.

- [`5fd6890`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5fd6890b5cf9b3400e00e4891a3a03084b82420d) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - What a look at the pass over the pages found, at the widths between a phone and a laptop and in the lists the rules keep.
  
  - A project's form no longer runs past its pane. The mark, the symbol, the colour and the image stood on one row of four from a window of 640 pixels on, which a pane holds only from about 830: in between the image's field was cut at the pane's edge, and a kept image's "Remove the image" widened the mark's column. The three fields now stand beside the mark one under the other, each as wide as the pane leaves, on a phone too, and on one row from 52rem on, with the way to take the image away under them.
  - A category's row stands on its shared columns from a window of 48rem on, where the page holds them, and wraps below that; from 640 to about 720 pixels the rows ran past the page and "Remove" was cut.
  - The feed's sentences start on one line down the feed again: the hour is written as the language writes one, a figure more or less from row to row, and its column has a width every row shares.
  - `--sc-label-case`, `--sc-gutter-wide` and `--sc-width-page` are among `CONSOLE_TOKENS`, so that a console that sets one reaches its plugins' frames with it and `/parts` shows it; a test holds the list to the stylesheet's root. A console that set `--sc-gutter` alone has its own gutter on a bounded page and the default one under a page as wide as its window, the board's: it sets `--sc-gutter-wide` too.
  - The Korean pack reads past the mark a question ends with when it chooses a particle, so a decision's question takes the form its last word asks for.
  - The feed's page names the feed's region once.

## 0.5.1

### Patch Changes

- [`1ef27be`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/1ef27be8d180fc193045d65f16834dc3798b4bac) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console use` writes the console's address into `.skillcdn-console.json` every time, where it wrote it only for a console that was not the machine's default login: a committed file with the project alone followed the default login of whichever machine read it, so that a teammate signed in to two consoles was refused, or wrote to a project of the same key at the other console. The account is written only where `--account` asks for it, or where the directory named it before, so that a committed file carries nobody's account unasked. A file from before is still read and means the default login; run `console use <key>` again in a checkout to have its file name the console (ADR-0112).

- [`3b6292d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/3b6292d9045f8db2086d22487526a3c2fc7ad7ef) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A picture chosen for a project's mark is shown on the mark before the form is sent. The pages' policy lets an image come from the console or from a `data:` address alone, and the form showed the chosen file by a `blob:` address, which the browser refused, so the mark kept its symbol until the project was saved; the file is now read into a `data:` address, where it is no larger than an icon may be.

- [`c29efbe`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/c29efbe193693af8d6211833d7f1102fe18e45e2) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - What a sub-agent is called, and the end of what a product wrote on its standard error, are cut after the account's secrets are out of them, as a tool's input is. A sub-agent's name was cut to 80 characters before, and a value that crossed the 80th kept its beginning in the transcript; the end of a product's standard error that a session's last error shows could begin with the end of a value whose beginning was let go. A session's driver is now handed `cutEnd` beside `cut`, which keeps the end of a text the same way, and `errorsShown` shows what a product wrote on its standard error through it, the last 4,000 characters; `createSpawn` keeps the last 16,000 and begins them with `…` where it let the beginning go. `cutShownEnd`, `plainCutEnd`, `errorsShown` and `MAX_ERRORS_SHOWN` are exported from `@skillcdn/console/runner` for a driver of one's own.

- [`78cc913`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/78cc913d8a00ee4f8263b399c4ea0b5b90cb79eb) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A decision's line says when it was asked, in `console work`, `console decisions` and `console decision <id>`: `(asked by Claude Code for carol, for bob, at 2026-11-15T13:52:30+09:00)`. A waiting decision holds a run, and nothing the command printed said since when. The moment of an answer and of what followed is printed in the workspace's time zone, as every other moment of the command's text is, where `console answer` and `console decision <id>` printed it as the API gives it, in UTC; `--json` is unchanged.

- [`cf352f4`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/cf352f42ed1feec57ee1c5a803139e6cd92cca7d) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console decision <id> --outcome` and `console decision <id> --to` work in a directory that names no project, where they answered that a project had to be said: the decision's own project is found from the decision, as `console decision <id>` and `console answer <id>` already reach a decision wherever it is. An agent account in its desk, woken by an answer with "Read it: console decision <id>", writes what followed from there. With a project named, nothing changes.

- [`f1289dc`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f1289dc3a2c80ac74e396f70202fb3177b0ff8b2) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A person has a desk too (ADR-0115, ADR-0122): a folder of their own that their agent begins in, with the instructions of an agent that works for them, their projects checked out under `work/`, and what should outlive a session or a machine. It has no runner and no `runner.json`, and is bound to no project. It is named as an agent account's is: the folder is `desk`, and the repository `<account>/desk` at the person's own git account.
  
  - **`console desk [<directory>] [--web]`** writes it for the person the command is signed in as: `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.gitignore`, each only where no file of that name is there, into `desk` in the current directory or the directory named. `--web` also writes the shell of the desk's pages into `web/`: the person's own extension of the console's pages, one empty page of their own, on the command's version of the package. Its last lines say what is next, in order, to an agent in that folder: the desk is set up as a repository with the skill for that in the organization's pack, and then shaped to what the person asks to keep and to see, with the skill `keeping-a-desk`. For an agent account, whose desk `console agent setup` settles, it writes the shell of the pages alone (`--web`); it is refused under a directory that works in a project.
  - **The template:** `@skillcdn/console/runner` exports `personDeskFiles`, the person's files, with `DESK_FOLDER`, the folder a desk is made in, and `deskWebTitleOf`, the title the shell of its pages is given.

- [`3f457df`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/3f457df56a2d6643df821c357d2f945912a10c47) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A desk is given its least, and grown by what its member asks (ADR-0122).
  
  - **The skill `keeping-a-desk`** ships with the package, beside the console's other skills: it teaches a desk's structure, its instructions and three places, each there once it is used, and how a desk is shaped to what its member asks to keep and to see: what is asked, how the shape of what is kept is chosen and written down, how a page of their own is added and what it is given, and what is checked before it is called done. Its page `collections.md` is the form for a set of things made with their uses and their figures, which a desk takes where its member asks for exactly that. For a page it names the shell's one page (`src/page.tsx`, at `/pages/mine`), the charts by their components and the ready blocks, a line each, and points at the package's README and the parts' catalogue for the rest.
  - **The skill `running-an-agent`** (1.17) and the terms of `working-the-board` say a desk as it now is: what is kept under `records/` in the shape that fits it, pages under `web/`, each there once it is used, and no `records/README.md`.
  - **A desk's files are the least.** `console desk` writes `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.gitignore` for a person, and `console agent setup` those and `runner.json` for an agent account: no file under `records/`, and no kind of thing to keep. Both `AGENTS.md` say the structure under "This desk": the repository is the member's own, committed and pushed when something is done; what is kept lives under `records/` in the shape that fits it, pages under `web/` and projects under `work/`, each there once it is used; the file says what the desk now keeps and shows, a line each; and something new is shaped as the skill `keeping-a-desk` says. A desk written before keeps its files as they are.
  - **`console desk` ends with what is next, in order,** as two numbered lines to say to an agent in that folder, or for the agent that ran it to go on with: the desk is set up as a repository, with the skill for it in the organization's pack; then the person is asked what they want to keep and to see, and the desk is shaped to it, as the skill `keeping-a-desk` says, named with its path on that machine. With `--web` it says that the pages are one empty page of the person's own until they say what they want to see. `--json` answers the two lines as `next`.
  - **Both `AGENTS.md` name `console whoami`** for where the skill working-the-board is, which says it in a line, where they named `console help`, which is read whole to find it.
  - **An agent account's desk takes the shell of its pages** from the same command: `console desk . --web`, in its desk, writes `web/` and nothing else, where it refused an agent account outright; without `--web` it still writes nothing. An agent account's `.gitignore` leaves out `web/dist/` from now on.
  - **`console help desk`** names the skill `keeping-a-desk` with its place, as `console whoami` names the board's.
  - **`console desk check`** reads the collections a desk keeps and leaves what else is under `records/` to the desk: a folder the index does not name is a problem only where it holds a `collection.json`, and a desk with no collection is told so, with exit 0.

- [`a2e3334`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a2e3334d04e0ccf2b61e1e0ba540a873949592b7) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A desk keeps what its member asks for under `records/`: plain files, JSON an agent writes with its file tools and the files themselves, which the desk's pages read. For a set of things made, with where each was used and how it did, the package has a form ready, collections (ADR-0117, as ADR-0122 amends it), which a desk takes where its member asks for exactly that. The format is `docs/specs/records.md`.
  
  - **The format's schemas** are exported from `@skillcdn/console/api`: `recordsIndexSchema`, `recordsCollectionSchema`, `recordsItemSchema` and `recordsItemsSchema`, `recordsUseSchema` and `recordsUsesSchema`, `recordsMetricRowSchema` and `recordsMetricsSchema`, with their types, their bounds (`MAX_RECORD_ITEMS` and the rest) and `readRecords(schema, text)`, which answers a file's value or every problem with it in words.
  - **The format is said to an agent** by the skill `keeping-a-desk`, on its page `collections.md`: the fewest words that let an agent write a collection right, with an example of each file. Nothing of it is written into a desk: `console desk` and `console agent setup` write no file under `records/`.
  - **`console serve [<dir>] [--port <n>] [--records <dir>]`** serves what a desk keeps at `/records/`, beside the build and the API, for the desk's pages: each file read when it is asked for, `GET` alone, nothing outside the directory; a picture, a video, a sound and a PDF by their kinds, anything else as a download; a part of a file where a player asks for one; a file git LFS has not fetched refused as `records.not_fetched`. Without the flag, a path under `/records/` is `records.not_served`.
  - **`console desk check [<directory>]`** reads a desk's collections and says each problem with its file and what is wrong, ending in 1 with any; a file that git LFS has not fetched is a note. It needs no sign-in. `checkRecords(files)` is the same check for a program.

- [`e84dd8a`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/e84dd8ae08588974bb2c92fe086fa090a661f7ce) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The Agents page says where a desk of one's own comes from: under "Connecting an agent", what to tell an agent for one, shown to be copied whole as the words for connecting are: the command's install, the connection to this console, and `console desk`, which the agent then follows to set the desk up (`DeskWords`, which takes the console's origin).

- [`5e32ec0`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5e32ec0f0ca9b2a75a36090aa7560493315f2205) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The package ships the shell of a desk's pages, `template/desk-web`: the four files of a console of one's own that opens on one page of the member's own, empty until they say what to show, built with `npm run build` and served with the desk's records beside it by `console serve dist --records ../records`. Two words in it are filled by the command that writes a desk: `__VERSION__` in `package.json` and `__TITLE__` in `index.html` and the README.

- [`eaf804d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/eaf804dfef31662f2da0ba9f8dbe765c0138a09e) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A desk's page (ADR-0116): `deskPage()` is a page for a console of one's own, at `/pages/desk`, a member's own beside the console's. Beside the desk's records it shows the member's work on the board across their projects in three views, each read from the console as it is looked at: what stands there now (`DeskStanding`: the decisions that wait, the open runs, then the open tasks by state), the board's figures (`DeskFiguresView`: done by week, how the runs ended, by project and by category) and the board's archive (`DeskArchive`, found by words, a page at a time). `DeskPage`, `readHistory`, `readOpenRuns`, `figuresOf`, `standingOf` and the bounds of a visit (`DESK_PROJECTS`, `DESK_TASKS`, `DESK_RUNS`, `DESK_WEEKS`) are exported with them. A page of a console's own is now given the query of its address, `search`, so that it can have views with addresses.

- [`f6aa458`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f6aa4587f7362696e9c0adb48a5b1a6540859476) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The ready page of a desk, `deskPage()`, draws its figures with the charts ([ADR-0123](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0123-charts-are-parts-placed-by-measures-and-drawn-by-the-stylesheet.md)). What it shows is as it was.
  
  - **A collection's results** (`DeskCollectionResults`): each metric and each ratio in all is a figure, a metric's with the run it came by; one metric the member picks is a chart by week, which carries its figures as a table for a reader who cannot see it; what did best is a ranking in bars, each item with its status and its figure.
  - **An item** (`DeskItemView`): its figures in all are figures, and its figures by day a table, a column a metric.
  - **The board's figures** (`DeskFiguresView`): what was done by week is a chart of columns; how the runs ended, and what was done by project and by category, are bars with their figures in words.
  - `BarList` and `FigureTable` take for a row's name what the page shows for it, a mark with a name or a badge, told apart by the row's `id`; `Chart` takes `figures="beside"` where the page shows the chart's figures itself, in a table.

- [`884d840`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/884d840ca41c68435ba72e299edfc753469008a7) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `keeping-a-desk` (1.2) holds the writing-down and the push until the member has looked at the page, and says that the ready view shows where an item was used on the item's own view; the package's README says what `DeskRecords` takes.

- [`906dcf6`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/906dcf603fccfac085ffdf7248cf086642ce8099) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console serve --records <dir>` serves a desk's pages before the desk keeps anything ([ADR-0122](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0122-a-desk-is-given-its-least-and-grown-by-what-its-member-asks.md)).
  
  - **A directory that is not there yet is no error.** A new desk has no `records/` until something is kept, and the shell's `npm run serve`, which is `console serve dist --records ../records`, ended there with "No records to serve". Now every path under `/records/` answers `404`, `records.not_found`, until the directory is there, and the line the command prints says that nothing is kept there yet.
  - **What comes to be kept is served with no restart:** the directory is looked for at each request.
  - **What is there and is no directory is still refused** before anything listens, as "is there and is not a directory".

- [`637d024`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/637d024fac0d674cc2e94bce42fae6b5987a844d) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A desk's page opens on the desk's records (ADR-0117): what its member made and what became of it, read from the files the desk keeps under `records/`, which `console serve --records` serves at `/records/`. `DeskRecords` composes the views, each exported and built from the console's parts: the collections with their items counted by status (`DeskCollections`), a collection's items as cards with their previews or as rows, narrowed by status (`DeskCollectionView`), an item with its files shown in place by the kind the server says each is, its fields, its links, where it was used and how it did by day (`DeskItemView`), and a collection's results, each metric in all and by week, the ratios, and the items that did best by a metric (`DeskCollectionResults`). What reads them is exported too: `readDeskRecords`, `readCollection` and `probeFile`, over the page's own `fetch` or a `RecordsReader` given to `deskPage({ records })`. A record was written by an agent: every file is bounded and parsed, what does not parse is named in a line and the rest is shown, every word is text, a link is taken only as https, and a file only from under its collection's `files/`.

- [`487e5c8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/487e5c8b19411251861916b82dc56ad006024d01) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The shell of a desk's pages, which `console desk --web` writes, builds quietly: `npm run build` says nothing of its own where it went well and leaves the pages in `dist/`, as its README now says. The bundler reported every file it wrote and warned of the size of the one script, each time, which is a screen an agent read for nothing and a warning with nothing to do about it on pages a machine serves to itself. The script is `vite build --logLevel warn`, and `vite.config.ts` sets the size warning past what the pages are; a warning of anything else, and every error, is printed as before. A shell written before keeps its script: change the two lines by hand, or write the shell again.

- [`d72fb72`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d72fb7261fceb5581e586da1e442e670d20b8dac) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The shell of a desk's pages is one page of the member's own, with nothing on it yet ([ADR-0122](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0122-a-desk-is-given-its-least-and-grown-by-what-its-member-asks.md)).
  
  - **`template/desk-web` no longer mounts `deskPage()`** (the template was never released with it). `src/main.tsx` lists the pages and mounts one, `page`, from `src/page.tsx` beside it, at `/pages/mine`, which `/` opens on. The page says that it is its member's and that their agent builds it from what they ask, in the language the pages speak; its comments say what a page is given, what it may read (the board as the member, and the desk's `records/` at `/records/`) and what it is built from (the parts' catalogue, and the charts). A desk written before this keeps the pages it has.
  - **`deskPage()` and its views stay exported**, as blocks one may add: `pages: [page, deskPage()]`. The README's "A desk's pages" lists them, each in a line.
  - **The README** has "Pages of your own" as a section of its own: what a page is given, what it may read beside the board, and what it is built from.

- [`21c75b4`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/21c75b4482796649198b3b74dfdff5994978b369) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The console reads its feeds no further than what has settled (ADR-0125). An event is numbered when it is written and seen when its change is committed, so a change numbered first could be committed last, and a reader that had gone past its number never read it: an agent account was not woken until the task's next event or its next briefing, a plugin's hook never heard of the event, and a page stayed stale until the next one.
  
  - **Writers:** whatever writes an event holds a lock, shared with the other writers, until its change is committed. Writers never wait for each other, and never behind a reader. The lock is on a table of the console's own, one a feed, in a schema nobody else may use: a plugin with a schema of its own cannot hold the board's writes or its feeds by taking it.
  - **Readers:** every read by number ends at the highest number below which no change is still under way: the board's wakes, the plugins' hooks on both streams, `GET .../events` with `after` or `latest=true`, the stream, a `--wait` for an answer, and `GET .../plugin-events`. An event committed while an earlier one is still under way is carried a moment later, in the order of the numbers.
  - **`503 feed.unsettled`:** a process of the console that has not seen a feed settle since it began, while a change is still under way, answers `GET .../events` and `GET .../plugin-events` with `503`, `feed.unsettled` and `retry-after: 1`, and not with an empty page. The pages ask again by themselves, a second apart; a script that reads the feed should do the same, and never take the refusal for a feed with nothing in it. What the API answers is otherwise as it was.
  - **A job no longer stops for good at one event:** an event whose wakes fail in twelve passes in a row is passed over, with an error in the log that names its number and kind; an event the console could not make ready for a plugin's hook is skipped after its tries with a `plugin.failed` event, as one whose hook fails is.
  
  A migration adds the schema `feed_order`, its tables `events` and `plugin_events`, which hold no row, and its function `writers_ended`; the console's database user makes it, as it made the transcript's schema. Writers that follow each other without a gap, a load of well over a hundred events a second from several writers side by side, hold the feeds where they are until they end; the console says so in its log after thirty seconds.

- [`edf21c4`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/edf21c434ccefcd41f36057275c1965a0920d160) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - On the front page of someone who has arranged nothing, a widget that leads with no widget beside it, the only one at the top or any lead on a phone or a tablet, is drawn at the size it is told, on that size's cells, where it took the page's width and four rows: a widget that comes in `small` and `medium` alone on a wide page was a box eight cells by four with a `medium` in it. The projects follow it in the columns it leaves, then across the page below it, and arranging the page starts from the same cells (ADR-0110).

- [`be0826d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/be0826d8acfaa4a1e9c1a8f3091bbd46f96ed1dd) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The lines the command prints for an agent to run expand nothing in a path, in bash or in PowerShell. The command that reads the next part of a skill, an outline's `--rows` and `--code` lines and the `cd` that `console-plugin init` says next quoted a path in double quotes, inside which both shells expand `$`, a backtick and `$(...)`: `console skill 'skills/$(x)/SKILL.md'` ended with a line that ran `x`. A path is now as it is where neither shell reads anything of it, in single quotes otherwise, and in double quotes where it holds a single quote and nothing either shell reads there; for a path with a single quote and a `$`, a backtick, a double quote, a backslash or a `!`, which no one spelling keeps whole in both, no line is printed, and the command says why and what to run.

- [`ff9922d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/ff9922d98549bc0f78e6916d2eb5902743e7eca0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - On Windows, reading the runner's log while the runner writes it no longer costs the logs put aside. A full `runner.log` held open by whatever read it, `Get-Content -Wait` say, could not be moved, and every line the runner wrote meanwhile let one more of `runner.log.1` to `runner.log.3` go until none was left. The task's shell now moves the log first, to `runner.log.0`, and moves the ones put aside only once that worked: a log that is held is added to as it is and put aside when it is let go. A machine set up before takes it once its files are written again, `console agent setup --refresh` in the desk, and its task is started anew.

- [`00da9f5`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/00da9f561774faa02616c9b33cb0206b70307c66) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console login` keeps nothing its credentials file would not read back.
  
  - What an address answered to begin a connection was kept as it came where the call ends before the approval, as under an agent. An answer beyond what the file reads (a wait over ten minutes, an empty code, a secret or a page's address too long) left a file the command could not read again, and every later command stopped on it, at every console signed in on the machine, until the file was moved away. The command now holds what it is about to keep to the file's own bounds, the connection that waits and the login a token becomes alike: where an answer is beyond them it says that the address answered what it cannot keep and stops with `1`, with nothing written, no code shown and no token given up for it. The file is never written in a shape it would not read back.
  - `restConnectionSchema`, what `POST /api/v1/connect` answers, is bounded no wider: the code up to 64 characters, the page's address up to `MAX_URL_LENGTH`, the secret up to `MAX_SECRET_LENGTH`, the instant up to 40 characters, the wait up to 600 seconds, none empty. A console answers a code of nine characters, a secret of some fifty and a wait of three seconds, so nothing changes for one; an answer beyond the bounds is refused as `invalid_response`.

- [`21910cd`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/21910cd9bb3f5e72b2b5cc11c6239ba34c6cd9ce) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console login` names the credentials file to a person at a terminal, and not to an agent.
  
  Once connected, the command ended with "The token is kept in" and the full path of its credentials file. Where no terminal shows the command's words, an agent reads them, and that handed it the path of the one file it must never open. There the line now says that the token is kept on this machine by the command, and never shown, with no path; the same for the session's secret of `console login --self` and for a token given with `--token-stdin`. At a terminal the line is as it was. `console logins` names no path either way.

- [`3d61a88`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/3d61a88c2df30d3b6455afb8844fe31c6bce5c1e) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console login` ends on what holds, not on a next step: "Only a project's checkout is bound to it: console use <key> (console projects lists them)." It said "Next, say which project this directory works in", wherever it ran, and a desk or a home directory was bound to a project because the line said so. The end of the wait for the person's approval is printed as every moment is, in the workspace's time zone where a login at that console kept it (`2026-10-09T19:00:00+09:00`), where it was the instant as the API gives it.

- [`51e5653`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/51e565332e955116b4597c4d4c9abe56b0075bf2) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: `console login` under an agent says its code at once, and a script that ran it with no terminal and waited in that one call for the approval now passes `--wait <seconds>` to wait there. Where no terminal shows the command's words as they are printed, as under an agent, whose host shows them when the command ends, the call that begins a connection prints the address and the code, with the line that says to tell the person both and to run the same command once more, and ends at once; the next `console login` for that console begins no new code and waits for the approval, ninety seconds a call, ending with exit code `3` and the words to wait on while the code lasts. `--wait <seconds>` says another wait for either call. At a terminal the command waits where it stands, as before. The connection that waits is kept in the credentials file while its code lasts and is never printed: `console logins` names its code, `console logout` forgets it. Nothing new is asked of the console, so a console from before this version is connected to the same way. A script that ran `console login` with no terminal and waited on it now says `--wait <seconds>`; `--token-stdin` is unchanged. The Agents page and the Connect page tell an agent to run the commands and do what each says.

- [`6b0b9ed`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/6b0b9eda06ecf50764e348f384162e73d350211c) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console login` under an agent says the telling first, and how long the next call waits.
  
  The call that begins a connection where no terminal shows its words ended with one sentence: tell the person the address and the code now, then run the command once more, which waits for the approval until a moment. A fresh agent started the second call first and told its person after, and read the moment, which is when the code ends, as how long that call waits, and gave it a timeout of half an hour. The words are now two lines after the address and the code:
  
  ```
  Tell the person that address and that code now, in a message of its own, before you run the command again.
  Then run console login --url <origin> again: it waits for the approval up to 90 seconds a call, and the code lasts until <moment>.
  ```
  
  At a terminal told `--wait 0`, where the next call waits while the code lasts, the second line says that instead: `it waits for the approval while the code lasts, until <moment>`.
  
  Nothing else of the command changes: the first call still ends at once with `0`, and the next still waits ninety seconds unless `--wait <seconds>` says another wait.

- [`819f219`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/819f219a3d5264cfbf452cbca14a4099f6deaedb) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent's conversation draws one line for an event that arrives, however long the session is. Every line of the session was drawn again on each event, so a session of a few thousand events slowed with every step the agent took; a line is now drawn again only when its own events, its own text read whole, its files or the day changed.

- [`8c490e0`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8c490e057463eecb7b2118d15189f8dac52678db) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console rules` and `console take` print the rules under a first line that says what follows (ADR-0119): `Rules <mark>:`, which parts there are and how many characters each is: what the project adds, its plugins, what the organization adds, and the console's rules, with who adds nothing said in a clause (ADR-0120). What differs now stands first and what always holds last, since the first is short and differs by project; the separate line "Rules of this project: none of its own" is gone, the first line says it. `console take <number> --rules <mark>` leaves the rules out where the mark is the one they have, with a line in their place, and prints them whole, saying so, where a rule changed since or the mark is of another project's; without it a take prints them whole, as before. `--json` carries the rules either way and gains `mark`.

- [`8889478`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8889478420edb337a244ec6ea4651ae04fd3dbad) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The partitions of a new month of the agent accounts' transcript are made whoever holds the transcript's tables. They were made with `create table ... partition of`, which waits for every other session to let go of the table it partitions: any role of the database, a plugin's with a schema of its own (`storage:sql`) among them, could hold it through a function of the catalog that takes a table's number, and the next month's partitions then failed to be made, leaving an agent account's sessions of that month nowhere to go. Each partition is now made as a table of its own and attached, which waits on no read, no write and no such role. An `analyze` of the transcript's tables by the database's keeper still makes it wait; the worker makes the month at its next pass.

- [`f2b7f75`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f2b7f756c8cf046bd57dff5de59450d6874babb7) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A part of a skill says what it lacks, and a page written is given with its address.
  
  - A part of a long skill that is not its last now ends, after the line that names the next part's command, with the console's own words: that the part stops in the middle of the skill, that its later steps, checkpoints and rules are in the parts that follow, and that the command is run now, before a plan, an answer or any other step, until a part ends without those lines. A fresh agent read one part of four, saw that the skill continued, and planned from that part.
  - The console's rules say the same in a clause: a long skill comes in parts, and every part is read before any is acted on, a plan included.
  - `console write` prints the page's address on a line `page: <address>`, as `console task` and `console decision` print theirs, so that an agent tells a person where the page is and hands it in.

- [`42d66e1`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/42d66e1017e8f59e965394910d555a9fa14a4985) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: a plugin's events are answered to each reader as far as they are theirs to read. `GET /api/v1/plugins/<key>/events`, which `console plugin <key> events` and the client's `plugin(key).events()` read, answered every event of the plugin to anyone who knew of it: the events of a private project the reader is not in, and every `plugin.command` with who ran it and its arguments. An administrator reads every one, as before. Anyone else reads the events of a project they may see, as that project's own feed shows them, and of the events that belong to no project their own doing, a `plugin.command` they ran, and what the console says of the plugin with nobody in it, `plugin.failed` and `plugin.upgraded`; a token reads as its person. What a reader may not read takes no place in a page and is not counted in `more`. Where the console answers none, the command says `No event of <key> that you may read`, and no longer that the plugin has none. A member who read another person's commands or an administrator's acts through this route asks an administrator, or reads the feed of the project they are in.

- [`2c0c8cc`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/2c0c8cc85cfa3c6d7773d72feec14f4e198908ea) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin's jobs are seen (ADR-0128). `GET /api/v1/plugins/<key>` answers `jobs`, each job the plugin declares with how often it runs, its last run and outcome, its next time and since when it has failed; `console plugin <key>` prints a line for each, and the plugin's settings page a row of them. A job that failed twice running is among the plugin's `problems`, `the job <name> has failed since <moment>`, in `console plugins`, `console plugin <key>` and the settings pages, where the list marks a plugin that runs with a problem, until the job's next run goes well. `console plugin <key> events [--kind <kind>] [--limit <n>] [--offset <n>]` reads the plugin's events, newest first, the failures alone with `--kind plugin.failed`, for a plugin with no board project as for any; `GET /api/v1/plugins/<key>/events` takes `kind`, and the client's `plugin(key).events({ kind })`. The command reads a console of before 0.5.1 as it did: such a console names no jobs and none are printed, and it does not narrow events by kind, so the command keeps the kind asked for of the page it read and says so.

- [`ab40fd5`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/ab40fd516742ce06d13bfc62ef054f6f5e83483c) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin with a schema of its own (`storage:sql`) works past the console's first restart where the console's database user is no superuser, as a managed database's is. Every boot draws the plugin's role a new password, and the statement that did so named every power the role must not hold, which only a superuser may name: from the second boot on, the plugin's schema could not be provisioned and its statements were refused. Now only a power the role holds is taken from it.

- [`6ad5892`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/6ad5892f603280a819abdf961c58c4ce80b02272) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: a plugin with a schema of its own (`storage:sql`) can no longer name a table of the console's (ADR-0133). Its role could use the schema `public` through `PUBLIC`, as every role of a new database can, and through it hold up the console's tables: such a plugin could make the board's reads and writes wait until they failed. When a plugin with `storage:sql` is provisioned, at enabling and at every boot, the console now takes from `PUBLIC` what it holds on the console's schemas (`public`, `drizzle`, `transcript`, `feed_order`) and on the plugins', and says so in its log (`PUBLIC may no longer use these schemas`).
  
  - **Every other role of the database** then uses the console's schemas only by a grant of its own: a role made to read the console's tables is granted `USAGE ON SCHEMA public` by name, by the database's owner.
  - **A console whose database user does not own the database** may not take that from `PUBLIC`: enabling such a plugin is refused with `plugin.unavailable`, and one enabled before is listed as failed, in words that give the statement the database's owner runs once, `GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA "public" TO "<the console's user>"; REVOKE ALL ON SCHEMA "public" FROM PUBLIC;`. A console whose user owns its database, as in the compose files, the smallest install and a managed database its master user made, runs nothing.
  
  A console with no plugin that asks `storage:sql` leaves its database as it is.

- [`c6be3b3`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/c6be3b3e1598c6920ee73d1675ea59f95e8a2563) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The password the console draws for a plugin's database role (`storage:sql`) no longer reaches the log or the plugin's `plugin.failed` event. Where the database refused the statement that made the role or gave it its new password at a boot, a role of the plugin's name that the console's database user may not change say, what was logged and recorded quoted the statement whole, the password in it. The password never took effect, but none belongs in a log. Now the plugin is refused with `plugin.unavailable`, in words that name the role and give the database's code and words, `the role "plugin_<key>" could not be changed: the database answered 42501, permission denied to alter role (...)`, and the statement is said nowhere.

- [`17e9e17`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/17e9e174112118ecfb6fb4c4991ac3bb09edf13d) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A project the console does not have, or does not show you, is said in full by every command that works in the project a directory's file or `CONSOLE_PROJECT` names, where `console rules`, `console tasks`, `console take` and the rest printed the console's bare `project.not_found`: the key, the console, what named the key, and the two things to do about it. Where the project is there and you are not in it, one of its owners lists you (`console member add <login> --project <key>`); where the key is wrong, the directory is bound anew (`console use <key>`), or the variable set to another. `console whoami` ends on the same two lines, in place of the hint to bind the directory to another project. A key typed with `--project` is refused in the console's own words, as before.

- [`7699776`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/769977679441527a8a8795eb951bc81207f70045) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A project on the front page of someone who has arranged nothing is an icon on one cell again. The projects now stand in rows as long as their column has cells, the last row ending short, and arranging starts from the same cells (ADR-0108).
