Under the bare heading an agent wrote a repository's own documents in the project's language, against the repository's agreement, which they follow. The example in the skill `planning-the-board` says both. Rules already written keep their heading.

- [`9224deb`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/9224deb027e593371ea4acf277cb1dd651ed01bd) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A command on a run (`report`, `hand-in`, `ask`, `finish`, `fail`) that finds no open run begun with its own token now looks for the open runs of the same person in the project, begun through another of their agents or before the command was signed in again, and names them in `run.none`: the id, the task, the agent it was begun through and when it was last heard from, with `--run <id>` to go on with one. It said only to take a task, which would have ended such a run, one waiting for a decision included. With none of the person's open, the words are as before. `console runs --mine` says whose its listing is, the runs begun through this agent, and how the person's others are listed, where it printed `No runs.` beside a run of theirs.

- [`a88a217`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a88a2175ecfe238f6c05b07e4c5361d8247a6c5b) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A runner that ends by itself, stopped by its service or for an update, no longer counts as delivered what its product had not taken: a signal handed to a product that was still starting, or at a turn, was counted when the runner ended, and the runner started after was never sent it. It is sent again now, and what the product did say it took, as it was being ended too, is delivered once. A supervisor's `restart`, `reset` or `stop` of an agent whose product had taken nothing of its session hands what that product was sent to the session that follows, where it was dropped. A session's record says so in a `status` that names the signals, and no longer shows a first message as read where the product never took it.

- [`a0bdf49`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a0bdf49aed6561f3831cb6c507f69fbbbe544da6) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - On Windows the runner's log is bounded. The task's shell adds the runner's lines to `runner.log` as before, and now puts the log aside when it holds four megabytes, as `runner.log.1`, keeping three put aside and letting the oldest go: sixteen megabytes at most, where the file grew for as long as the machine ran. A machine set up before takes it once its files are written again, `console agent setup --refresh` in the desk, and its task is started anew.

- [`f8fd0b6`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f8fd0b66fc568533fbc86755bb786df99e1f8464) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin's `secret` setting may keep one value per key (ADR-0111), for a token per installation of a service: one per workspace an app is installed in, say. Declare it with `keyed: { max }` (1 to 1,000 keys). A key is letters, digits, `_` and `-`, up to 64, and no secret.
  
  - **Sealing:** `ctx.fetch`'s `seal` takes `key`, a path into the same answer (`$.team.id`) or the key itself. Two outcomes are new, and in both the value is withheld: `no_key` (the answer holds no key there) and `too_many` (a new key past `max`).
  - **Placing:** `secret: { name, key, as }` puts one key's value into a request.
  - **`ctx.secrets`:** `has` and `status` take a key. New: `keys(name)`, `forget(name, key)`, and `move(name, key)`, which puts the value kept from before the setting was keyed under a key, for an upgrade.
  - **For an administrator:** the settings page, `console plugin <key> settings`, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/v1/plugins/<key>/secrets/<name>/keys/<key>`, and `console plugin <key> secret <name> --key <key>` list a keyed secret's keys and set or remove one at a time.
  - **Records and tests:** `plugin.configured` names the key as `under`. `createTestContext` takes a keyed secret's values by key.

- [`7bad057`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7bad0576b9bb4ce3fbe64cfac08a49784a7d2e9a) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A runner's line no longer says that a session's events are "kept and sent again later" where they are not: a session that ends while the console takes no more of its events within the hour, or while the console is away, is let go with what it had not sent, and the runner says how many events were let go and why (`3 events of session <id> were let go unsent when it ended: ...`).

- [`32c8dba`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/32c8dba8e2d904e8fafba2e3b5708fd4c20c2e0d) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console agent setup` prints the end of its wait for the account's sign-in as every moment is printed, as `console login` does: in the workspace's time zone where a login at that console kept it (`2026-10-09T19:00:00+09:00`), where it was the instant as the API gives it.

- [`2d02aa1`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/2d02aa143e5623c85196499d746203e4cad8fbd9) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A runner counts a signal as delivered when its product took it, and no longer when it handed it over: a product that did not start, followed by the runner's own end, lost what it was sent, since the runner started after began past it. `delivered`, in the runner's file and in its heartbeat, is now the last signal the product said it took, the record says was never read or let go, or the runner acted on itself, with every one before it; what a product was handed and has not said it took is sent again to a runner started after. The agent's page and `console agent` count the same: "3 of 4 delivered" while the fourth waits to be taken.

- [`58ba445`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/58ba445e295c370f32cf9698e670134ccfc576ad) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The page of the skill working-the-board on reading the pack (`references/the-pack.md`) puts the search first: a skill is found by words, `console skills --search <words> --limit <n>`, and then loaded; the listing of the whole pack, which is as long as the pack is, is for looking around it. Its table began with the listing, and agents listed a whole pack to find one skill.

- [`ce529e7`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/ce529e7ef405c52773667d72c8c01661bc4d96af) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console skill <path>` takes a skill's folder as well as the path of its `SKILL.md`: `console skill engineering/skills/review` reads `engineering/skills/review/SKILL.md`, and `.` the root's own. Where the pack answers that no skill, or no file, is at a path, the command says that, with the path it read and how the pack is listed, in place of the words about a private pack and its token, which are now kept for a pack that serves nothing to the console. After a listing and after what `console skills --search` found, a line says how a skill is loaded whole, its rules first: a skill read with `--file` comes without the rules of its pack.

- [`8456aac`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8456aac3248e1bdd9c8d68d864111bf33b37a46e) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Where `console take` is given a mark (`--rules <mark>`), the line that answers it stands directly after the line of the run's ids, before the task: that the rules are left out, or that they are not the ones marked, in which case they follow whole after the task, as the line now says ("They follow whole after the task, under the mark they have now."). It stood after the task, where a session that cut the take at its head never read that a rule had changed. Without a mark nothing moves.

- [`84e9346`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/84e9346ea75636855476abbc76267fa914e3ce62) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console task new <title> ... --take [--rules <mark>] [--agent <name>]` writes a task and takes it, in that order, in one command: it prints what `task new` prints, the number first, and then what `console take` prints, with `--rules` and `--agent` as `take` has them; `--json` carries what a take answers with the task as it was written beside it (`written`). It is for work a person gives in chat, which is written and taken at once: a session that sends its commands together could not read the number between the two, on a board where another agent writes too. With `--take` the task is written `ready` where no state is said; a state that cannot be taken (`done`, `dropped`) is refused before anything is written, and so are `--rules` and `--agent` without `--take`. Where the take fails after the task was written, the command says so with the number, `console take <number>` being the way on, so that no second task is written; where the run began and only the reading of what a take prints failed, it says that the task is taken and by which run. The skill `working-the-board` (1.36) uses it for work given in chat. Without `--take`, `task new` is as it was.

- [`05b5f26`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/05b5f266669ec457e7863a43116feb7429e48748) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A runner cuts a text its driver shows a part of, a tool's input above all, after the account's secrets are taken out of it, so that a secret at the cut is never left in part: one, two or three characters of it, a hexadecimal value, a key's line. A driver is handed what cuts (`StartOptions.cut`, the type `Cut`, and `plainCut` for a driver run without a runner) and cuts through it; `cutShown` of `@skillcdn/console/runner` is what the runner hands it, which looks through the beginning of a text alone, so that a megabyte of input is not read for a line of four thousand characters. A driver of your own that cut a text by itself goes on working, and shows a part of a secret where it did before, until it cuts through what it is handed.

- [`ae21b7f`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/ae21b7f787c196663988e9dd78159e920d206299) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Under the `claude` driver a thinking block the product sends with no words, its signature alone, no longer becomes a `thinking` event of the session's transcript, a sub-agent's included. A run under the runner had 34 such events of 37 thoughts, each showing nothing on the agent's page and each counted against the events an hour of a session takes.

- [`5605ad0`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5605ad0e80cf6e12b287b0bee001747d93b8e3c5) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: an agent account that signs in at a git host is held to more than its login at its first sign-in (ADR-0126). Where the deployment names its organization at the host, the console asks it, as the account is added, which of its members the login is, and binds the account to the host's id of it: that account alone is let in as the agent account, whatever it is called by then, and whoever else comes to carry the name is refused. Where the organization has no member called so, or cannot be asked, the account is added not confirmed, and its first sign-in is let in only where the organization has the login as a member then; an account added before this release that has not signed in yet is held to the same. Where the deployment names no organization, the account is let in by its login as before, and says so. An agent answers `firstSignIn` (`account`, `member` or `login`; `FIRST_SIGN_INS`, `FirstSignIn`) while it waits for that sign-in; `AgentStartSteps` takes it and marks an account not confirmed; `console agent new` and the account's line say it. The words `agents.page.start.account.at.gh` are three, by what the sign-in is held to, where they were one.

- [`df5a217`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/df5a2170593929f425109cb459f12dcc31c4f059) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account's desk says what lets a session, and a subagent it starts, begin a task at once (ADR-0115). The `AGENTS.md` that `console agent setup` writes now says that the desk is bound to no project, so that a board command run there names one (`--project <key>`) as a wake does; that a project's repository is cloned under `work/<name>` and its `AGENTS.md` read before the first change there; and what a subagent is handed, the task by its number and its project, the checkout and the skill `working-the-board`, with the run taken once and named on every command after. It names the workspace as it is called, which read doubled where that name ends in Console.
  
  Both desks' `AGENTS.md` say what a desk is for (ADR-0117): what was made and should be found again is kept under `records/`, the kinds named in the file; what is done in the desk is the member's own and on no board unless they put it there; the skill `working-the-board` is read before the first task of the board, and not for a job that is no task; the desk is never bound to a project. A person's says which language to write to them in, where the console knows one. An agent account's says that the organization's pack is looked in before a job is done by hand, that a brief names a task and does not restate it, and that scratch files go under `work/`. Each lost what an agent knows anyway.
  
  A desk that is there keeps its `AGENTS.md`, under `console agent setup --refresh` too: an account set up earlier writes the lines in itself.

- [`39c1492`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/39c1492652ce875e85e31882ce2713866d26c7f4) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console tasks --search` that finds nothing says how it looked: every word given must be in a title, so a search of several words is told that fewer find more. The board's skill has an agent look for work given in chat by one word a title would hold.

- [`868cef8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/868cef80062a7ca1c39954dde29a993243dd5c9b) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A session's transcript says which events a sub-agent made. Under the `claude` driver an event of a sub-agent carries two facts, `parent`, the id of the tool call that started the sub-agent, and `subagent`, what that call called it; the conversation on the agent's page shows such an event as a step under the sub-agent's name, what the sub-agent said among them, folded as a thought is, so that the agent's own words are told from what it had done for it. The `codex` and `acp` drivers' products do not say whose a line is, and their events read as before.

- [`7fe31c7`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7fe31c70ccbed9e6473de12ced9b8bb3d6155b68) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: `console ask` does not wait at the terminal where the command is signed in as an agent account, unless `--wait` is given, and ends at once with exit code `3`: nobody attends an account, and the answer wakes it. It says so, with the run to go on with when the answer comes, where it told an attended agent to keep waiting, and exits with `3` at once. A person's agent waits as before.
  
  `console agent secrets <handle>`, asked by the account of its own handle, is refused in words that name the commands that are the account's own, `console secrets` and `console secret <name>`.

- [`90f4f40`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/90f4f40aaa5fc63831b86c248845f52e865f710e) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking (the colours of a series, below): charts, as parts of the console's look, for a page of a desk and a plugin's page alike ([ADR-0123](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0123-charts-are-parts-placed-by-measures-and-drawn-by-the-stylesheet.md)).
  
  - **Eight parts** in `console.css`, each with its row in the catalogue (`src/styles/README.md`): `stat`, `bars`, `chart`, `spark`, `share`, `legend`, `figures` and `series`, the colour a series is drawn in.
  - **Their components**, exported from `@skillcdn/console`: `Stat` and `Stats` (a figure with its name, its change and its run), `BarList` (a ranking or a comparison), `Chart` (columns or lines over time, one to a few series against one scale), `Sparkline`, `ShareBar` (one whole in its parts), `Legend` and `FigureTable`. Each takes its figures as props, says what it shows to a reader who cannot see it, and is as wide as where it is put; the README's "Charts" has their props and which is for what.
  - **`@skillcdn/console/charts`**, a new entry with no dependency: the measures the charts are placed by (`chartLayout`, `chartScale`, `columnOf`, `barLengths`, `shareParts`, `sparkPoints`, `labelStep`, `labelledPoints`, `seriesClass`, `chartMeasure`), for a plugin's plain bundle. A measure is an attribute of an SVG and never a style, so the charts are drawn in a plugin's frame as on a console's page.
  - **Breaking: the colours of a series have other values.** `--sc-series-1` to `--sc-series-6` are now blue, teal, raspberry, coral, violet and green, chosen so that any two of them are told apart, also by a reader who does not tell red from green, and so that each stands out on a light ground and on a dark one. Slots 2, 3, 4 and 6 are another hue than before (they were orange, green, ochre and teal); a plugin that drew with these tokens is drawn in the new colours.

- [`178e59a`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/178e59a317d0368d448f3b4c6069a2cd435e47b1) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console docs <folder>` takes a folder as its own listing prints it, with the slash after it (`console docs notes/`), where it refused that spelling as no folder's path.

- [`2c0c8cc`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/2c0c8cc85cfa3c6d7773d72feec14f4e198908ea) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: `events` is a subcommand of `console plugin <key>` (ADR-0128), so no command or query of a plugin may be named `events`: `definePlugin` refuses one, `console-plugin check` says so, and a console of this version does not load a plugin that has one. Rename the command or the query; none of the plugins published beside the console has one. `capabilities`, a subcommand since consent was moved there, is refused as a name the same way: a command so named could never be reached.

- [`8a5d754`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8a5d754edec2d603a90498b16a5dc28881976853) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console fail` and `console abandon` say the state the task is in, read from the console once the run is over: `Run <id> failed; task #<n> is in_review now.`, `Run <id> abandoned; task #<n> is ready now.` They said unasked that the task was up for review, or back as the take found it, which a console of 0.5.0 does not do, nor one of 0.5.1 where a person moved the task meanwhile or another run is open on it: the task is `in_progress` still there, and the command now says so. One request more (`GET .../tasks/<id>`), not made on `--json`, which stays the run as the console answered it. Where the task could not be read, standard error says that the run is over and is not to be ended again, then what the reading ended on, and the command exits with `1`.

- [`dfff72d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/dfff72d8845d03896bed8b57112eb068b94dc9e0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console finish` says the state its task is in, read once the run is over (`Run <id> finished; task #<n> is in_review now.`), as `fail` and `abandon` do, where it said the task was up for review without asking: a finished run leaves its task at work where another run is open on it.

- [`d709025`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d709025eae394ecb481fe50cfa2e1d505e4fa5bf) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console logout` forgets a connection that waits at a console nobody is signed in to, and nothing is kept anew with one whose code ran out.
  
  - Signed in at one console with a connection waiting for a person at another, `console logout --url <the other>` refused with "Nobody is signed in here" and left that connection's secret in the credentials file. It now forgets the connection, says so and ends with `0`; with nothing waiting there it refuses as before.
  - Whatever the command keeps anew carries no connection whose code ran out: `console whoami` and `console workspace`, which keep the workspace's time zone on the login, wrote such a connection back with its secret.

- [`734135d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/734135d3f04e07388060e6da5fcd79db7109e109) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: `GET /api/v1/me` tells nobody signed in the workspace's name and its language alone, which is what the sign-in page shows. For nobody, `tokenDaysAtMost` is `null`, `skills` is `{ "source": "", "address": null }`, and `peopleVisibility`, `weekStart`, `timeZone` and `skills.packToken` are left out, as the short name, the domain and the services already were. A member's answer is as it was, and the schema is unchanged: a console built on an earlier version of the package reads both.

- [`a4c6454`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a4c6454735692c5548e45c72780d1a13a7bdf12c) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: the end of a run leaves no task at work behind it (ADR-0114). A run that ends as `failed` now puts a task that is `in_progress` up for review, as a finished one does, for a person to look at why; a run that ends as `abandoned`, left by its person, given up on by an owner or ended by the console after twelve hours, puts the task back in the state the take found it in (`ready` for a run begun before this release). A task a person moved meanwhile, and one another run is open on, stays where it is. Until now both left the task `in_progress` with nobody at it. `console fail` and `console abandon` say what became of the task, and a task the console itself gave back wakes no agent account. A migration adds `runs.task_state_before`.

- [`0ba9b1e`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/0ba9b1ed46bb09f2b779f0fea26bbca942100a96) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `migrate reseal` says how many values the previous secret opened, and says so in words where it opened none. A secret given as `AUTH_SECRET_PREVIOUS` that is not the one the deployment had opens nothing, and the step answered `left: 0` as a finished rotation does, which read as leave to take the real secret out. The line `sealed again` now carries `openedByPrevious`, and where that is `0` while some values open with neither secret, a second line follows, `the previous secret opened no value`, saying to check that `AUTH_SECRET_PREVIOUS` is the old secret, given in the same form the old one was, before it is taken out. The exit codes are as they were. `deploy/README.md` "Rotating the secret" and the skill `deploying-a-console` say the same.

- [`8af0e7b`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8af0e7bb3465d525d89d54690564cf6956df027b) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console rules write --workspace` writes nothing on a console of before 0.5.1. The command sends what the organization adds, and a console of 0.5.0 keeps what it is sent as the organization's whole rules, in the place of its own: the console's rules stopped holding there, under the words "Wrote what this organization adds". The command now asks the console for its rules first (`GET /api/v1/rules`, one request more), and where the answer says them whole and without `own`, as a console of before does, it writes nothing, says that the console is older than 0.5.1 and is updated first, and exits with `1`. What `console rules` prints of such a console no longer names `console rules write --workspace` as the way to write the organization's. A project's rules are written as before: they added to the workspace's at 0.5.0 already. `none` puts the organization's away as before, which leaves the console's alone on either console.

- [`bd6ca97`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/bd6ca97b3495df0647a29cc45a2b999dde8dfe11) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console serve` carries the files of a plugin's frame, so that a plugin's pages and widgets are drawn on a console of one's own as on the organization's: a `GET` of `/api/v1/plugins/<key>/web/<version>/…` or of `/api/v1/plugin-runtime/…` is carried whatever page asks, with no token, since a frame is sandboxed and its origin no page's, and the console answers those files to anyone. Until now they were refused (`auth.forbidden_origin`) and a frame stood empty or unstyled. Every other path under `/api/` is carried for the console's own pages alone, with the token, as before, and so is any other method on those paths.

- [`6f2bcf9`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/6f2bcf956b815d37957aa74bf51cb168ab03966e) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The Agents page says little of a desk ([ADR-0122](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0122-a-desk-is-given-its-least-and-grown-by-what-its-member-asks.md), point 6): a title, one line, and the block to tell an agent, with nothing after it.
  
  - **The block** (`DeskWords`, which takes the console's origin as before) is "Run these in order, doing what each one says before the next:" over three commands: the command's install, `console login --url <origin>` and `console desk`. It names `console desk` alone: a desk's pages come when its member asks for them, and what follows the install is the command's to say, in its own last lines.
  - **Gone from it:** `--web`, the paragraph on what a desk holds, the paragraph on what the agent does, and the words on a login that is there already.
  - **The pack's words** under `desk.command` are `title`, `line` and `quote`, in English and Korean.

- [`cbb9654`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/cbb96540df8fd94c247f875ec4f6d3433782283a) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The skill working-the-board (1.34) says what a second round of runs by agents new to it missed. Work of a project given in chat stands in the skill's opening, as what holds unless the board's rules say otherwise: the rules read, the work looked for on the board with `console tasks --search <words>`, the task taken where it is there, else written as one task with its category, its number told, then taken. On a project the console does not show, the agent tells the person the key, the console, what named it and the two things they can do, and binds the directory only to the key the person or the repository's agreement names, saying so. Before a task is closed on a person's verdict, what the run's summary named as left is said. A decision an earlier session asked and that still waits is told to the person at once, and not waited for at the terminal. A skill of the pack is found by search. A later take of a session carries `--rules <mark>`, the mark the rules were printed under, and the output of a take is never cut to skip the rules.

- [`d04dac3`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d04dac34c7e944a276750d17e70bec9e07549aa6) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The skills working-the-board and planning-the-board, with their references and the rules every skill of the console shares, lose what an agent has anyway, and keep every instruction: working-the-board, planning-the-board and its page of setting up are each shorter. Gone: the summary of the phases above the phases, what a console is, how `console help` is read, the links to the decisions behind each rule, what the board's rules say themselves (a problem reported as a problem, who writes the rules), what a command does unasked, the colours the help of `category` lists, and, on the page of setting up, how an agent account is run, which is the skill running-an-agent's. No command a run is worked with is taken out.

- [`db85dc5`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/db85dc548aacdb35b291d2d5e34774c9546c21cc) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A project's page opens its live stream again after the console restarted. A browser gives a stream up for good once it is answered something that is no stream, a gateway's error during a restart say, and the board then stood still until the page was loaded again; the stream is now opened again after the last event that arrived, a few seconds later and further apart while the console does not answer, as an agent's conversation is.

- [`5a96edf`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5a96edfce13c96d92d627184e2fcbc6d1bb6549f) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The command prints the address of a task's page and of a decision's, on a line of its own (`page: https://console.example/projects/web/tasks/12`): `console task`, `task new`, `task edit` and `take` for a task; `ask`, `decision` and `answer` for a decision, in the project the command reached it in, else at the decision's own address. An agent that tells a person of a task or a decision gives that line, in place of the console's front page. The address is made from the console's origin and the pages' routes and asked of no console; `--json` is unchanged. `console decision <id> --wait` that runs out, on a command signed in as the agent account that raised the decision, ends in the account's words, that the answer wakes it, as `ask --wait` does.

- [`435af3f`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/435af3f5068fab4fd80504de2c84b45e6678ba11) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: the console's rules always hold, and an organization adds to them where it rewrote them whole (ADR-0120). No organization had written its own, and a rewritten whole would have stopped following the console's version. An administrator now writes what the organization adds, read after the console's rules and before a project's; an addition holds where it differs from what stands before it, and none takes back what the console's rules say never happens.
  
  - `GET /api/v1/rules`, and the workspace's half of a project's rules, keep `body` as what holds whole, the console's rules and then the additions under the heading `# What this organization adds`, so a command of 0.5.0 prints it as before. `source` is `reference` where nothing is added and `written` where something is; `page` is the additions' page. Two optional fields carry the parts apart: `own`, the console's rules, and `added`, `{ body, page }` or `null`.
  - `PUT /api/v1/rules` writes the additions. A body that begins with the console's rules, the whole a client of before sends back, is written as what follows them; with nothing following, the additions are put away.
  - The package exports `CONSOLE_RULES` in place of `REFERENCE_RULES`, with `rulesWith`, `addedRulesOf` and `ADDED_RULES_HEADING`; `isReferenceRules` is gone. `RulesForm` takes no `onReset`, and at the workspace it edits the additions alone, empty until something is added, with the console's rules read above it; `RulesView` shows the console's rules as they hold and the additions below them. The pack `rules` has `console`, `added`, `nothingAdded`, `add`, `readConsole` and `form.hintAdded` in place of `reference`, `writeOrganizations` and `useReference`.
  - `console rules` and `console take` print the parts with what differs first and what always holds last: what the project adds, with the plugins' lines, then what the organization adds, then the console's rules, under one mark over all of them. The first line says which parts there are and how long each is, and in a clause who adds nothing. `console rules write` writes what a project adds, and with `--workspace` what the organization adds; `none` puts what was added away. A console of 0.5.0, which answers the workspace's rules whole, is printed as two parts.
  - A page written before as the organization's whole rules reads as an addition from now on.

- [`5fc231f`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5fc231f8e7672aa8c2e5466a50edb8f9f0784f43) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The deployment's secret can be replaced without losing what the console sealed (ADR-0127). The image of this version reads `AUTH_SECRET_PREVIOUS`, the secret the deployment had before: what it sealed is still opened (the pack tokens, the plugins' secrets, every value of an agent account's store, a sign-in in flight) and nothing is sealed with it. `migrate reseal`, a word of the `migrate` role run by hand once the new secret is rolled out, seals again under the current secret every value that only the one before opens, and ends with one line, `sealed again`, whose `left: 0` says that `AUTH_SECRET_PREVIOUS` can be taken out. Nobody is signed out by a rotation: sessions and tokens are kept as hashes. `deploy/README.md` "Rotating the secret" has the three steps, and the skill `deploying-a-console` does them for the reference deployment ("The console's own secret replaced"), where it said before that the secret was replaced only at the cost of entering everything again.

- [`54e5b70`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/54e5b70931700e1361da33660c11e99d861a50ce) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The first page of a desk's shell is called "Desk" on its tab, since it is what a member's desk shows; the ready page (`deskPage()`), which a member may add beside it, is called "Records and work" after what it shows, so that the two never share a name.

- [`04fb1c3`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/04fb1c315fe56d7c67813f669676e72870c6730b) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The lists of tasks and of runs have a brief form: `GET /api/v1/projects/<key>/tasks?brief=true` answers the tasks without their bodies, and `.../runs?brief=true` the runs without their reports, counted in `reportCount`. The client has `taskBriefs` and `runBriefs` for them, with `restTaskBriefsSchema`, `restRunBriefSchema`, `restRunBriefsSchema` and `reportCountOf`. The command asks for the brief form wherever it prints a line a task or a run (`tasks`, `runs`, `task`, `goal`, and the look for the open run every command on a run makes), and whole on `--json`; a desk's pages read their board views in it (`DeskReader` is `taskBriefs` and `runBriefs` now, and `DeskRun` carries a run without its reports). A console from before this version ignores the word and answers whole, which reads the same.

- [`3a71c5a`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/3a71c5a38dd0ab35d54cb27ed9e4db07ab242846) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account's onboarding task says what the first account left alone with it got wrong.
  
  - **A run that waits stays open.** The task told the agent to finish "with what is done and what still waits on a person", and a run finished with decisions waiting could ask nothing more. It now says that a run waiting for a decision stays open, that the answer wakes the account and it goes on with that run (`--run <id>`), and that the task is finished when nothing of it waits on anyone.
  - **The account's own address.** Where the account signs in to the console with an address, the task names it as the address an invitation to the messenger comes to; where it signs in with a login at a git host, the task has it ask which address, and offer none of its own making. `onboardingOf` takes the account's `login`, and `agentAddressOf` says the address; a service module is handed it as `address`.
  - **The desk's item names the organization's pack,** where a skill may set a desk up: `console skills --search "desk setup"`.

- [`7ef6c40`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7ef6c40b25793e3c9f11c51015798404775e4d2d) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console skills` and `console skill` read the organization's pack in a directory that names no project, where they answered that a project had to be said: an agent in a desk, or in a folder a repository is about to be made in, finds and loads the organization's skills with no `--project`. The answer begins with a line that says whose pack it is; `--rows`, `--code` and `--json` carry none. The console serves it at `/api/v1/workspace/skills` and, under it, the reads a project's pack has, for every member of the workspace, with the workspace's SkillCDN token where the pack is private (ADR-0113); a console older than this answers that it does not, and the command says to name a project. The client gains `organizationPack()`, which answers a `PackClient`, the seven reads a `ProjectClient` and a `PluginClient` have.

- [`d3d19fa`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d3d19fa69609804e7a43c834f3ab596c10c927dd) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The package is built on SkillCDN 0.5.2. `console skill` joins the pages of a skill that includes several files into one text per file: read in several pages, such a skill came out with each file's heading repeated with nothing under it, and sentences broken across the seams. A console that serves a pack from a directory on its own machine goes on answering while a large directory is indexed.

- [`8b33d28`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8b33d28749e1d13c4faf6195828fcdde4841eaa7) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - While the front page is arranged, "Reset to default" puts it back as it is composed for someone who has arranged nothing: every width unarranged, every tile taken off back on the page. Done keeps it, as a `home` of `{ hidden: [], layouts: {} }`; Cancel lets it go. The button cannot be pressed where the page already stands so (ADR-0109). Both packs carry the new word, `home.reset`; a pack of a console's own adds it.

- [`04a571e`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/04a571e4091124a40bbeb7d47e46279f9fa06206) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: `console-plugin check` holds the `sc-` classes of a plugin's web bundle to the console's parts (ADR-0106). It reads every script and stylesheet of the bundle, the entry, its chunks and its styles, and fails on a class of one of the console's own pages, which lays that page out and changes with it; a name in the console's prefix that nothing of the console styles is a warning. The parts are the rows of the catalogue the package ships, `src/styles/README.md`, read where the command is installed, so a part added there is one a plugin may use. A plugin built from the parts, as the template and the example are, passes as before.

- [`8d44278`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8d442781e5da902715c71ccbbbcd995375e53c48) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console-plugin check` reads a file of a plugin's package up to 4 MiB, and in a time that grows with its size. It read every file whole, whatever it weighed, and three of its readers tried again from every place they could have begun: a stylesheet of 240 KB of comments never closed took seconds and one of some megabytes minutes, as did a script with an `import` followed by nothing but blanks, and a name in the console's prefix of 8 MB ended the check on an error of the runtime's. A file beyond the bound, a manifest, the server module, a script or a stylesheet of the web bundle or a skill, is now a problem said by its size, `<file> is <n> bytes: the check reads a file of up to 4 MiB (4194304 bytes) whole, and did not read this one`, and is not read, the server module not loaded either; `console-plugin help` says the bound. A plugin's files are a fraction of it, the example's server module under 1 MiB. The stylesheet's comments, the names in the console's prefix and the imports of a bundle, `forbiddenImportsIn` of `@skillcdn/console/plugin` among them, are read once from end to end and find what they found, with one difference a browser makes too: a comment opened and never closed takes the rest of the stylesheet with it, where its classes were read before.

- [`16c6c70`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/16c6c70493c3afb00320a795eb84c83239784218) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: the command reads a pack with the reads of the `skillcdn` command itself (ADR-0124), and a long skill comes in parts.
  
  - `console skill <path>` prints a skill of more than 28,000 characters in parts, so that the host running your agent shows each part whole: a host may save a longer output to a file and show only its start. A part says at its head that the skill's context is incomplete, and ends with the command that prints the next part, `console skill <path> --cursor <cursor>`, and a line that says to run it now, with `--project`, `--url` and `--account` as you gave them. Read every part before using the skill. A skill that fits is printed whole, as before, and `--json` carries it whole.
  - `console skill --file <path>` prints a page of 24,000 characters, ended at the end of a line, unless `--limit` says otherwise, which a host shows whole too. `--json` carries the page the API gives, 40,000 characters unless `--limit` says otherwise.
  - A plugin's skills are printed with the plugin's own commands named in them, `console plugin <key> skill` and `console plugin <key> skills`; they named the project's.
  - `console skills --rows` and `--code` ask the console what the pack is before they read, one request more; what they print is the same.
  - A number out of its range is said as `skillcdn` says it: `--limit takes an integer between 1 and 200`, without the value after it.
  - `@skillcdn/cli` is a dependency of the package now, for those reads: an install is some 10 MB larger, since that package declares what its own command serves a pack with. The command alone imports it; nothing of it reaches a console you build from the package.
  
  Nothing changes in the REST API, and the command reads a pack through a console of 0.5.0 as before.

- [`1b5f762`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/1b5f762db4e7daef84fe7b4edb59c7a615fb9405) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: the command's reads of a pack go as the `skillcdn` command's own do (ADR-0118), where they had drifted from it.
  
  - `console skill --file <path>` prints a page of the file, 24,000 characters, ended at a line, unless `--limit` says otherwise (up to 100,000), and where the next begins, which `--offset` reads; it printed the file whole.
  - `console skills <folder|file>` prints a page of the folder or of the outline, 50 entries unless `--limit` says otherwise (up to 200), and a page that ends before the folder does names its `nextCursor`, which `--cursor` continues; it printed the folder whole.
  - A read the pack refused ends with exit `1` and the refusal on standard error, the listing too; it was printed with exit `0`. A path with nothing at it is said in the pack's own code and words (`file.not_found: ...`), a skill that is not there with the skills there are; `--json` still prints the console's answer, which says the status.
  - `console skills --search` takes its folder with `--path <folder>` as well as after the words.
  - `console skills --rows` takes `--format json`, an array of rows with their cells by column name; `console skills --code` takes `--grep <pattern>` and prints each block under `--- <path> > <heading> (<language>) ---`; `--out` says `Wrote <how much> to <file>.`; a document with no table prints the CSV's header alone.
  - The listing marks a folder's README as the overview of its folder.
  
  The tables and the code blocks are still joined; a long skill comes in parts (ADR-0124).

- [`584b171`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/584b171da08cab522ac7df426a4d9f595e07a650) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The ready desk page (`deskPage()`) is composed as an example of a page of figures built from the parts: the few numbers first, then one chart, then what breaks them down, and no figure twice. `DeskCollectionResults` shows each metric and ratio in all, then the one metric the person picks, by week as one chart (columns for what is counted, a line for a share) beside the items that did best by it; the table of weeks under four small charts is gone, the chart carrying its own figures for a reader who cannot see it. `DeskFiguresView` leads with the tasks done and the runs by how they ended as figures, then what was done by week, by project and by category. `DeskItemView` shows how an item did, with the run each metric came by, before where it was used and its files, and its figures by day last. A view with nothing yet says what would fill it and draws no empty axis. The props of the three are as they were; the words `desk.records.week` and `desk.figures.runs` are gone from the language packs, and `desk.records.noFigures`, `desk.figures.lead`, `tasksDone`, `ended` and `empty` are new. `keeping-a-desk` says the order a view of figures is laid out in.

- [`3e154e1`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/3e154e10db23dc9616e23ff62126e9b7af9bfbe4) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The refusal of a project the console does not show words its second case as what is done and by whom, where it handed the reader a command: "Where the key is wrong, the directory is bound to the right one, the key the repository's agreement names or the one the person says, with console use <key>", in place of "bind this directory anew: console use <key>". An agent told to do as it saw fit had run the command at once, bound the checkout to another project and gone on with no task.

- [`b566fff`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b566fff9857dab539cc4d44ba9e3d675fed28610) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The console's rules say that work given with no task is written and taken in one command (`console task new` with `--take`), where they named `--state ready`.

- [`68b9bdc`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/68b9bdc373eccf6c37e6bc953552039aacc54ebc) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The console's own rules, which hold on every board, say four things more and two things less. A task written from chat is written and taken in one command (`--take`); in a project several repositories share, a task names its repository and is worked in a checkout of that one; work found on the way that the task does not cover is a task of its own, named in the report; and a skill of the pack is looked for by search before a job is done by hand. What the skill working-the-board and the command's help say already is no longer repeated there: what `console task <number>` prints, and how a host with a connection of its own names a skill.

- [`3ed6467`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/3ed64672ce47691eb1e4931e3aa8ccdcf3b40c12) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The console's own rules are written anew, and are shorter by two fifths: about 3,600 characters, from 5,981, printed with every take. They hold what an organization could decide otherwise, in the order an agent meets it: before work (a project's work has a task before it is done; a member's own desk is on no board unless they put it there; act as the member and no further), while working (where the work lives, the repository's own agreement, work found on the way, a skill looked for by search), asking, reporting, and what never happens. How the board is worked is the skill working-the-board's and a command's use is its help's, so the rules no longer say what `console take` prints or how an answer is written, and name a command only where it is what the rule is about. A project's rules say what differs in the project and nothing else, five things at most: where its work lives, the skills to load first, its pages' folders, who decides what, and its language where it is another. `PROJECT_RULES_TEMPLATE` has those five headings, and the example in planning-the-board takes that shape.

- [`079a778`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/079a778cad483e6239609c865c125f4d28c7c640) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The sentence `console use` prints for a checkout's agreement says to check: "**Bound to a console?** Before the first step, run `console whoami`: when it names a project, or `.skillcdn-console.json` is in this checkout, work the task through that SkillCDN console (its answer says how)." The one before named the two conditions and no command, and an agent in a checkout bound to a project its console did not have never ran `whoami` and began the work with no task. It ends on what `console whoami` answers, which names what to read, and on nothing else to read. A checkout that carries the older sentence stays right; copy the new one when its agreement is next changed.

- [`3b3faa8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/3b3faa8b1f44de2b8f02a236fe7d620cf0a83443) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The skills working-the-board (1.32) and planning-the-board (1.6), which the package carries, are brought to what the command and the board now do, and say each thing once. New in them: how a task's state is read before it is taken, and that a failed run goes up for review while a run given up gives the task back; how a review's verdict is written when a person gives it to their agent (`console task edit <number> --state done`, or a comment and the task taken again); the right project checked with `console whoami`, which says what named it and ends with `project.not_found` where the console does not have it; a desk and a new folder naming no project, so that commands there say `--project <key>`; a task of a project several repositories share worked in its own repository; work found on the way written as a task of its own; and `console use` binding a checkout, on the page of setting up. Gone from them: what `console help <command>`, the board's rules and `SKILLCDN.md` own and they repeated, the exit codes and the filters of a listing among it. The example task is written `--state ready`, as the skill says every task is.

- [`88f709a`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/88f709ac80bca2241311056914df562d6073be79) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The console's rules are known in a text that carries them whole as they were read, so that only what was added is stored. `addedRulesOf`, and with it `PUT /api/v1/rules`, knew the whole only as the package keeps it: with `{language}` filled, as `console rules` prints it and a command of 0.5.0 sends it back, or with lines that end in a carriage return and a line feed, nothing was cut, and the console's rules were stored as an addition and read twice from then on. The whole is now known with any words where the language stands and with either line ending, wherever it stands in the text: what was added before it and after it is kept, without the heading. A text that quotes a sentence of the rules, or a part of them, is kept as it is.

- [`0b0321d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/0b0321d7242f500e6331ec75eed1733ed77361f0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Two sub-agents of one kind at once are told apart in a session's transcript. Under the `claude` driver a sub-agent was called by its kind alone where the call named one, so two `Explore` sub-agents were both "Explore" on the agent's page; it is now called by its kind and what the call says it is for, the kind first (`Explore: Read src/ of acme/web`), within the 80 characters the name keeps, and by the one of the two where the call names only one.

- [`678f97a`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/678f97abe673093bd844c3208394c294bccb76fe) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - What an agent account's runner writes to the console is bounded by the hour: the events one session's transcript takes (60,000), the sessions an account begins (60), the wakes its runner raises itself (600), and the changes of its state the workspace's feed is told (20). A console refuses one more with `transcript.too_many`, `transcript.too_many_sessions` or `signal.too_many` (`429`, with `retry-after`), and keeps a change of state beyond the bound with the account, telling the feed how the account stands once the hour has room. The runner holds back what was refused and says so once: a session's events are kept and sent again after a minute, then after twice as long each time, a quarter of an hour at most (`HOLD_MS`, `MAX_HOLD_MS`); what a wake source raises is let go for as long; a session that could not be begun is tried again after the cool-down. The bounds are `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_EVENTS_PER_HOUR`, `MAX_AGENT_SESSIONS_PER_HOUR`, `MAX_RUNNER_WAKES_PER_HOUR` and `MAX_AGENT_STATE_CHANGES_PER_HOUR` of `@skillcdn/console/api`. Work meets none of them; a product that fails again and again does.

- [`39c1492`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/39c1492652ce875e85e31882ce2713866d26c7f4) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - What a fresh run of a desk given its least asked: the Agents page's block has the agent do what each command says before the next, the login saying its address and its code itself; `console login` prints the end of a first sign-in's wait in the machine's own time zone; `console desk . --web` in a desk that is there says nothing of what is next; the package's README says what the readers of a collection answer and what the small parts take; and `keeping-a-desk` (1.1) leaves the page up for its member to look at, makes no sample to fill it, and sends an agent to the README before the types.

- [`083a9da`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/083a9da3883e45e67b671ee48b0c64904fe1a3a1) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The skill working-the-board (1.33) and the console's own rules say what runs of agents new to them were missing. A person's own open run is gone on with and never taken again, read with `console run <id>` and named with `--run <id>` where another of their agents or sign-ins began it. A task named to be taken is read once: `console runs --task <number> --open` says whether a run is open on it, and `console take` prints the task. When the console shows the person no such project, the agent says what they ask for: an owner to list them, or the directory bound anew. Work longer than a sitting reports before it is finished. A review's comment keeps what the person said apart from what the agent adds. An agent account does not wait for an answer and does not finish around a decision: the run that asked stays open and is gone on with when the answer wakes it. The rule that work given in chat goes on the board first is a rule for a project's work: what a member does in their own desk for themselves is on no board unless they put it there, so no task is written for it and nobody is asked whether to.

- [`6950655`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/69506552d1f31fb1517b67787e937c400a7cefc6) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - What a second round of fresh agents asked of a desk's files and its check.
  
  - **A person's `AGENTS.md`** says that what changes in the desk is committed and pushed, without asking: a fresh session committed a record and asked whether to push, twice.
  - **A search names a limit** wherever a desk's files and the command tell an agent to search the pack: `console skills --search <words> --limit 5`, in both `AGENTS.md` files, in the sentence `console desk` prints and in the onboarding task's desk item. One run spent 26 KB on finding a skill that was the first hit.
  - **An agent account's `AGENTS.md`** says that each subagent keeps its scratch in a folder of its own under `work/`.
  - **`console ask --wait <n>` as an agent account** ends a wait that ran out in the account's words, that the answer wakes it and it goes on with other work, where it still said how an attended agent keeps waiting.
  - **The onboarding task's end** says that an item is set up or was answered as not to be done now, and that the last report lists what is not done and who said so: an item a manager answers with "Not now" was neither set up nor left waiting.
  - **`console desk check`** notes a use or a row of figures dated before its item was made, as `note: …`: most often a day written wrong, and no problem, since only the member knows. Notes are bounded as problems are. It notes an item that carries no status where its collection names some, too.
  - **The page on collections** (`collections.md` of the skill `keeping-a-desk`) says that every item of a collection with statuses carries one, that each state an item can be in is a status, and that a rate of two metrics is a ratio, without which no rate is shown: two fresh runs left items without a status and a collection without a ratio.

- [`0cbd0bf`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/0cbd0bfb13e8df73231a703230f51531ff4fc502) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: `console whoami` asks the console for the project the directory names, where it repeated the key it read, and ends with exit code `1` where the project is not there or not the person's to see: the line says the project's name beside its key and what named it, `.skillcdn-console.json` with the directory that holds it, `CONSOLE_PROJECT` or `--project` (`; project web (The web app), named by .skillcdn-console.json in /work/app`). A project the console does not have, or does not show you, now ends the command with exit `1` and `project.not_found`, after the lines that say who and where: a script or an agreement that checks a checkout's binding with `whoami` no longer passes on a key alone. `--json` gains `projectName` and `projectSource`; `project` is the key, as before. A directory that names no project is as it was (ADR-0112).

- [`b4b2261`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b4b2261586f8f1553aef13ea9dc55c440eb2b795) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The second line of `console whoami` says what is read before the first task and what one command's flags are read from: "Before the first task, read console rules and the skill working-the-board; for one command's flags, console help <command>. The skill: <path>". The sentence for a checkout's agreement now ends on "(its answer says how)", this answer, where it ended on `console help`: an agent that had the skill read the whole help beside it, a third thing nothing asked for. The path of the skill stands last on the line, with nothing after it.

## 0.5.0

### Minor Changes

- [`5d84030`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5d840307709725d32a4d8c5b8aaa1207e03f28a2) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The console on SkillCDN 0.5: every `@skillcdn` package is taken at 0.5.1, and this is the console's 0.5.0 (ADR-0034). What a 0.5 deployment says more of a pack is passed on as every answer of a pack is, and printed by the command: the section of a document a reference's fragment names, with where to read it; in a document's outline, how many rows each heading's tables hold and how many tables and code blocks the document has; a document made from another marked as derived among what a search finds; and how a search was ranked, by identifiers and by how much of the pack is embedded. A pack's own check holds the links' fragments now (`fragment_not_found`), which the console's pack passes. A pack read from a directory is read under 0.5.1's rules: a page a read cuts ends at the end of a line, a name written with underscores is found as written, and a question about a document is answered with the document where a skill used to stand first.

### Patch Changes

- [`f572246`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f5722469a9ae95c9ee62f99d5d713d503fc29573) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A badge may say that what it names is at work this moment: `Badge` given `live` carries a dot of its hue before its words, a ring spreading slowly from it, and a dot that stands still for whoever asked their device for less motion (`sc-badge-live`). An agent account that is running says so this way, on its page and on its card in the list; every other state reads as it did.

- [`f1756cf`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f1756cfedde71520404517964abd8e7aa604ca24) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - On the month's calendar, a goal's mark no longer pushes the bars of its own day a row down: every day of the week keeps the rows the day with the most goals has, so that a task lying across the week is one level line and its title crosses neither the tasks of the days after nor the goal itself. The count beside the workers' pictures ("+2") stays on one line where a row is narrow.

- [`10bec77`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/10bec771318d21feb039c22feeb4a6535c3e542b) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: a class the stylesheet has no rule for is no longer on the elements, and a rule nothing named is gone. Off the elements: `sc-agent-accounts`, `sc-agent-card`, `sc-agent-elsewhere`, `sc-agent-form`, `sc-agent-secrets`, `sc-approval`, `sc-connect-code`, `sc-decision-header`, `sc-fieldset`, `sc-member-lookup`, `sc-pack-token-form`, `sc-paged-more`, `sc-people-organization`, `sc-people-page`, `sc-plugin-frame-box-widget`, `sc-plugin-holder`, `sc-plugin-holders`, `sc-rules`, `sc-service-fields`, `sc-sessions`, `sc-settings-words`, `sc-sign-in-button-label`, `sc-talk-working-what` and `sc-workers-field`. Out of the stylesheet: `sc-decision-people`, `sc-decision-row-answer`, `sc-plugin-grant-input`, `sc-project` and `sc-report-list`. A stylesheet of a console of your own that named one of them names nothing now; the parts it can name are listed in `src/styles/README.md`.

- [`6182a36`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/6182a360d82daedeb0053ed509d43a405ae54385) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account's page holds its conversation to the session shown, and says what it could not do. A text read whole is kept with the session it was read in, where it was kept by the event's number alone and so stood in place of the event of the same number once a restart or a reset began another session; the events before the first one shown are read once at a time and put in once. The box to speak in keeps its words and its files where the console refuses a tell or the request fails, with what went wrong under it, and is emptied once the tell was taken; before, it was emptied as it was sent. A session's stream that closed for good, as it does while the console restarts, is opened again from the last event read, a few seconds later and further apart while the console does not answer, and the conversation says at its end that the connection was lost once it has been for longer than a moment; before, the conversation stood still without a word until the page was loaded again. What the record names in a word of its own is said in the page's: the kind of a signal sent, what a tool's question was answered and the names of a note's facts read from the language pack, and a word the pack has none for stands as it came. Going from one account's page to another's begins a page of its own, with nothing of the first in the second's fields. `useAction`'s `act` answers whether the change went through.

- [`eac8eff`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/eac8eff06dfab649cdbdc4f79b1b975912cd7406) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The form of a SkillCDN token, a project's and the workspace's, says whose token it may be: one a person made on their account page for the pack's repository, or the one a deployment's operator made for a pack of the deployment's own directory, which a deployment of SkillCDN 0.5 may require of every reader. Nothing else changes: the console reads no meaning into a token, tries it against the pack once and keeps it sealed, as before. The skills `working-the-board` and `planning-the-board` say the same, and that no code connects such a token, since nobody signs in at a deployment that serves a directory.

- [`5d1f318`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5d1f318edca919440c496da84724b44e8279e171) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A file a supervisor sends with a tell is written in the desk under a name every system and every product takes as one file: a comma becomes `_`, since a product handed pictures by their paths reads one as the end of a path, and a turn with such a picture never started; a name Windows keeps for a device (`NUL`, `CON.txt`) gets `_` before it; and two names that differ by the case of their letters alone are numbered apart, as two of a name are.

- [`6933f9c`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/6933f9c4c5fab57f4abcd91bf888c5825f5e9b34) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Under a finger, a drag on an agent's conversation that stands at its end, or at its beginning, moves the page on, as a finger expects of a page: on a phone the pane is nearly as tall as the window and held every drag for itself, so that a finger put down on it reached nothing else of the page, the box to speak in below the window's foot among it. With a mouse the pane keeps its scrolling to itself as before.

- [`1b87193`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/1b87193235c6a779d04ba37bb419bfc32d8331ea) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Two forms say why they will not go on. "Add an agent account" says that the personas are on their way while they are asked for and, where they could not be read, that the name and the handle are written by hand: an account is added without a persona, the console drawing its picture, where the form could not be sent at all before and said nothing. `AgentAccountForm` takes `proposalsFailed` for it. The form of the workspace's services says under the field what keeps it from saving: a short name or a domain that is none, a service that may not be named for its role, a value the service does not take or one that is not written as it takes it; `serviceProblemOf(role, service)` answers that as a fact (`ServiceProblem`) for a page to put into words, beside `serviceProblem`, whose English words for a program are what they were. The services as they stand name a value by the page's word for it, in the page's language. Saving an agent account's picture lets go of the file's address a while after the press, where a browser that begins a download a moment later found nothing, and says so where the picture could not be written out.

The navy ramp is a lighter denim, so that it no longer sinks into the ground. No set was released, so no picture held changes.

So the owner and the workers of a task, whom a decision is for and who answered it, who wrote a comment, whose run it is, who set a goal, a member of a project or of a team and whom a role went to. - [`198fce0`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/198fce0223b017ec1d347f11408932c67273b54e) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The account an agent signs in with at a git host has to be the organization's before the agent account is added, and everything that names that account says so: the form, the first step on the account's page, the setup guide, `console agent new` and the skills `running-an-agent` and `planning-the-board`. An account added and not used is deactivated, which takes its listing away, and the skill says so.

- [`2413f69`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/2413f694f02411a9748afa2a0ac2ea41af0b7474) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account carries a mark after its name wherever a member is named short: "AI" in a small frame, with "Agent account" on hover, on a task's owner and workers, on who a decision is for and who raised or answered it, on a comment's author, a manager, a member of a project or a team, and among the people a form offers to choose from.
