For whoever builds a console: `KindMark` is the part, `PersonChip` and `PersonOption` carry it, and the words are `people.kindMark.agent` and `agentTitle`.

- [`bd40813`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/bd40813d0341bcaa3cfd6c129191e490ce4abfb2) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A member is made an agent account only where that is asked for (ADR-0104). `POST /api/v1/agents` naming a login a member already signs in with is refused, `409` `agent.login_is_member`, where it made that member the account for good and said nothing: a proposed persona's login, put together from its handle and the workspace's names, may spell a colleague's. `restAgentInputSchema` takes `convert`, `true` to say that the member is to become the account; `console agent new` takes `--convert`, and without it says the two ways on; `AgentAccountForm` finds the member among the people it is given (`memberSignedInAs`, exported), says who they are and that it is for good, and sends nothing until the box beside those words is ticked. An administrator and an agent account are refused as before, whatever is asked. A language pack of your own gains `errors["agent.login_is_member"]` and `agents.accounts.form.member`.

- [`f6eda17`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f6eda1705b74a5ee0160a7345e36118f7c636662) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - What an agent account was sent is written on its session's record when its product takes it (ADR-0098): at once where the product waited for a message, and after the tool call under way or with the turn after where a turn was under way, so that the record reads in the order the agent read. What a session ends on before the product took it is written with `unread: true`, and the record says where a turn ends, a `status` with `turn: "completed"`. An `interrupt` may carry no words: it then stops the turn under way so that what the agent was sent and has not taken is read now, and sends nothing of its own; `console agent interrupt <handle>` takes none too, and `POST .../signals` asks words of a `tell` alone. For whoever writes a driver: a `DriverEvent` of the type `taken`, said once for every message sent, when the product reads it; a driver that says none has its messages written when the turn completes.

- [`d914ee6`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d914ee6ce206569749ead6a99a3c6cfb6fc47f49) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A pack's tables and code blocks are had as data through the console (ADR-0100). `console skills --rows [<folder|file>]` prints the tables of a document, or of every document under a folder, as one CSV, every page joined; `--code` prints the fenced code blocks as written, each under a line that says where it stands; `--heading <words>` keeps what stands under a heading, `--out <path>` writes a file and says how much it holds, and `--json` gives what the console answered. A read as data that the pack refuses fails on standard error, so that a redirected file is never the refusal. The console answers them at `GET .../skills/rows` and `GET .../skills/code`, for a project's pack and for a plugin's, with the token the pack is read with; the client has `skillRows` and `skillCode`, the schemas `restSkillRowsSchema` and `restSkillCodeSchema`. A document's outline names these reads, where it named nothing of them: `console skills <file>` says the command for each.

- [`a0d7673`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a0d76738102d3f1c0bc9c786d2ff1c552431c182) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: the picture overlay measures by the picture's own size. A picture longer than the window was cut at the window's edge, top and bottom, and could be neither moved nor made smaller, so that the rest of it was never seen. Now a picture opens with all of it in view; one much longer than the window opens as wide as the window has room for, from its top, and one much wider as tall as there is room for, from its left, so that what is shown can be read. It zooms out until all of it is in view and in to eight times its own size, the figure in the bar being the scale against its own pixels (100% is a pixel for a pixel) and the way back to all of it; a double press looks at its own size where it was pressed, and from there shows all of it again; a drag, the arrow keys, Page Up, Page Down, Home and End move it as far as there is more of it. A press on the picture no longer closes the overlay, the keys are heard after a drag as before it, and a long file name no longer widens the overlay past a phone's screen. For whoever builds a console: `zoomAround` and `panBy` take the picture's size and the stage's; `wholeScale`, `openingView`, `boundView`, `LONG_PICTURE` and the type `PictureSize` are new; `clampScale`, `FITTED` and `MIN_SCALE` are gone, since a scale of 1 is the picture's own size and no longer the fitted one. `Lightbox`, `PictureThumb`, `usePicture` and `PictureOverlay` are used as before.

- [`db48a94`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/db48a944c15ce3acc97a9b8c6cc16e82f133780a) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin's own events are read once a page, not once a frame: the frames of a plugin on the page, four widgets of it on the front page as much as one, share one reader, which asks `GET .../plugin-events` every few seconds while a frame listens and the page is in view, one ask at a time, and asks nothing while the tab is in the background, asking at once when it comes back; the stream is read from where it stands when the first frame listens, which the answer now says as `newest`, the number of the plugin's newest event, so that finding it is one request and never replays what came before.

- [`5c46cdb`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5c46cdb77319ed95d387c91a00f2fc0347af76a8) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A console of this version runs a plugin's `upgrades` with `ctx`, as a job has it: the `migrate` role refused them every call, so that no upgrade that read its records ever went through, and the install stayed at the data version it had. A plugin whose records are behind its code says so in `problems`, which `console plugins` prints; it is loaded all the same. The skill `building-a-plugin` (1.8) says what an upgrade reaches, and that the code reads a record of the version before as well.

- [`be67cad`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/be67cad01a0fb3ba6ab89005900c3317c0af4afa) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A project on the front page comes at a glance of its board too, as `medium` 4×2, `large` 4×4 and `wide` 8×2 (ADR-0105): its mark, its name and three figures, the tasks in progress, those in review and the decisions waiting; under them, or beside them on a wide tile, its tasks in progress, the soonest due first, its goals ahead with their days and their bars, and its tasks waiting for review. The rows are fitted to the room the tile has, each whole, with how many more there are, and parted by hairlines. A glance is read in one request (ADR-0107): `GET /api/v1/projects/<key>/glance`, `client.project(key).glance()`, answers the board counted by state, the decisions waiting and the rows, ordered and cut by the console with how many there are in all (`RestProjectGlance`, with `restProjectGlanceSchema`, `RestGlanceTask`, `RestGlanceGoal` and `GLANCE_ROWS`), so that a tile's figures and its rows are of one moment. `HomeGrid` takes the answers as `glances`, by the project's id, and asks for them through `onGlance` with the projects it shows at a glance, an empty list when it shows none; `ProjectTile` takes one as `glance` (`ProjectGlance`). `createGlanceReader` reads them for a composition of your own: once for each reading, again when asked, a failed read tried again and a late answer dropped. `glanceFit`, `glanceProjects`, `isGlanceSize` and `GLANCE_REM` are exported with them. The default console reads the glances each time the front page is shown and when the person comes back to its window, and the projects with them (`actions.refreshProjects()` of `useConsoleData`). The tile is one link named by the project and its figures. `ProgressBar` given `words={false}` is the bar alone, a picture of figures said beside it.

- [`b99e04d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b99e04d04c49749554125cda5d59c41a9d865382) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A `reset` of an agent account gives what its product was handed and never took one more try, in the new conversation, and no more than one. What a product does not start with is kept and sent again, which is right for a sign-in that was mended; a signal the product could never start with was sent again with every signal after it, through every restart and reset, until someone ended the runner on its machine. Where the product does not start with it again after a reset it is let go: the session's record says which signals, the runner says so in a line, and whoever sent it says it again where it still matters. What a `restart` keeps is as before.

- [`0b3fd9c`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/0b3fd9cda23a3e42276cd66fb53fb00866ae3f8d) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A runner takes an update of the command by itself (ADR-0101). At every heartbeat it reads the version of the package it runs from; another version than its own, seen twice, is an update: nothing more is handed to the product, the turn under way ends in its own time, the open session ends as `restarted` with a line on its record that says why, and the runner ends with exit code 0, for the service that keeps it to start it again on the new command. The next session goes on with the conversation, and what was sent meanwhile is sent again from the console's record. A runner the console refuses as too old tells its agent, once, the command that installs the console's version the way this command was installed, where it only asked the agent to tell whom it reports to. `createRunner` takes `installed` and `updateCommand`, both optional, and `run()` answers how the runner ended: `stopped` or `updated`.

- [`bbf8ec7`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/bbf8ec773be1bd55005aca4fdfb4e4ec1f7567de) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - In an agent account's conversation, the word that says a tool's call is still running stays on one line where the row is narrow, and a session's end is said once, by the note's own words, where it was said again as a fact beside them.

- [`59b58ea`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/59b58ea9b58904cbdbd84a15e3396f27105d2060) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A `tell` and an `interrupt` carry files of any kind, up to eight (`MAX_SIGNAL_ATTACHMENTS`), where they carried four pictures (ADR-0095). A picture is still one by its bytes and the one kind the console shows in place; any other file is kept as what its sender called it and answered as bytes to save, and a file called a picture that is none is `application/octet-stream`. The runner writes every file of a signal in the agent's desk, at `work/inbox/<number>/<name>`, and the message says each on a line of its own after the signal's words: where it is, or that it could not be handed over. A picture is handed to the product as a picture too where the product takes one; where it takes none, the agent is told to open the file, and the event of what it was sent names the pictures the product was not shown (`unseen`) beside the other files (`files`). The `codex` driver takes pictures, each as `-i <path>`: a picture sent to an agent that runs Codex was left out unsaid before. For whoever writes a driver: `Picture.path`, `Driver.can.imageFiles`, `DriverSession.images()`, and a runner's `files.write` takes bytes; `attachedLine` and `deskName` beside `composeSignals`.

- [`db74793`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/db74793e3a661c513023852567d34161438a07e9) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The Tell button on an agent's page says when the agent reads what is told by what its product takes: after the tool call under way where the product takes a message in a turn, and when the turn ends where it takes none, as Codex and an agent over the Agent Client Protocol do (`offers.steer` of the runner's heartbeat). The command's help says what is so: `console help runner` names the `acp` driver among the three, `console help agent` shows `--login` of `agent new` as what it is, left out where the workspace names its identity service, and says of a `reset` that nothing else begins a new conversation for an agent that could not go on. The skills `running-an-agent` and `planning-the-board` say the same.

- [`c522eb2`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/c522eb2ecb80547cf9c695ed4e27395daa17eae5) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A member has a title (ADR-0084): a line that says what they do (`Release engineer`), a person's as an agent account's. `PATCH /api/v1/people/<id>` takes `title`, from an administrator, and of an agent account from its supervisors too; `console person title <login> "<title>"|none` writes one, and `console people` prints it after the name, with an agent account's handle and standing. The People page shows it under the name and lets whoever may write it in place, marks an agent account with a badge and says "invited" until its first sign-in; the member's page says it under their name; the pickers of people show it as a second line, the login where none is said (`PersonOption`, and `renderOption` on `Picker`). A plugin reads `title`, `kind` and `handle` with the people, and its hooks receive `person.updated` as they do the other people events. For whoever builds a console: `supervisedBy`, `PersonOption`, `PeopleListProps.onChangeTitle` and `mayChangeTitle`.

- [`b99e04d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b99e04d04c49749554125cda5d59c41a9d865382) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console runner` sizes a batch of the transcript as it is sent, a text counted as JSON spells it: output full of newlines, quotes or escape characters made a batch the console refused as too large (`request.too_large`), which the runner then sent again every second for as long as the session lived, so that the Live page stopped and what was unsent at the session's end was lost. A batch the console still refuses as too large is sent again in halves, never the same one twice, and an event that is too large alone has its text cut, with a word that says so, then goes without its facts, then is let go. What waits while the console is away is bounded by its bytes too, 64 MiB with its pictures beside the 20,000 events: beyond either the oldest are let go, and one `status` event in their place says how many, where they went without a word. The runner says once that the transcript waits and once that it is sent again, and says a heartbeat the console does not take and a stream of signals that ends once for as long as it lasts, where it wrote a line a second. `createRunner` takes `bounds`, for a test to lower.

- [`ea78fc0`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/ea78fc0b084591cf864c1304f2dc57950edd057a) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A decision that waits can be addressed anew (ADR-0096): the people named take the place of those it was for, so that a question an agent asked of the person it works for is passed to the colleague whose answer it is, and nothing is raised twice. On the pages, a waiting decision has Change who decides, wherever it is answered: its project's decisions, its task, My decisions and its own page. `console decision <id> --to <login|me>`, given once for each person, does the same from the command. Anyone who may work in the project may, as anyone may answer; an answered decision stays as it was addressed, and a plugin's as the plugin addressed it (`decision.plugin_addressed`). The feed says who passed it to whom (`decision.addressed`, with `deciders` and `decidersAdded`), and an agent account named anew is woken. For whoever builds a console: the client's `project(key).addressDecision(id, deciderIds)` (`PUT .../decisions/<id>/deciders`, `restDecisionDecidersSchema`); the data's `addressDecision`, `addressDecisionIn` and `membersOf`; `DecisionCard`, `DecisionList`, `TaskView`, `TaskThread`, `MyDecisions` and `ReachedDecision` take `addressing` (`DecisionAddressing`: the people to name, given or read when the form opens, and `onAddress`); the words `decisions.changeDeciders`, `addressNobody`, `addressHint` and `peopleNotLoaded`, and `feed.event.decisionAddressed`.

- [`16bde0c`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/16bde0c91339a8a2b5aba09f1a4d143a8b11ea1c) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The line round a warning is a token, `--sc-color-warning-line`, the clear amber the warning callout and the warning badge wrote as a colour of their own: a console that sets its own tones sets this one with them, and a plugin's page is sent it with the rest (`CONSOLE_TOKENS`).

- [`f4908d4`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f4908d4b7dab5f09dfee37d1c3f370172e9fe275) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account's supervisors are the administrators and the reporting line above it (ADR-0084): an agent of `GET /api/v1/agents` says `supervised`, whether the asker is one, and the listing answers the accounts the asker supervises beside the ones they see. Where a workspace narrows whom its members see to the people they share a project with, a member sees their own reporting line besides, whom they report to and whoever is below them. An agent account is never an administrator, the one rule of the policy that reads `kind`; on every other rule it stands where a person does.

- [`7b85986`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7b859860c7b77fa91981e403ca4036205894a36f) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account is a member of the directory (ADR-0084): `GET /api/v1/agents` lists the workspace's agent accounts with whom each reports to, `POST /api/v1/agents` makes one for an administrator, from the login it will sign in with, its handle, its name, its title, the parts of its avatar, its manager and the project it starts in, and `GET /api/v1/agents/<handle>` reads one. The member's row is written at once, so that work is assigned to it before anyone signed in with it, and its first sign-in binds the provider's account to the row; a login a member already carries is made an agent account the same way, once. A person carries `kind`, `handle`, `title` and `avatarParts` in every answer, and `invited` while nobody has signed in with an agent account. An agent account is never an administrator (`person.agent_admin`) and always has a manager (`person.manager_required`). For whoever builds a console: `restAgentSchema`, `restAgentsSchema`, `restAgentInputSchema`, `agentPath`, `REST_ROUTES.agents`, `isHandle`, `RESERVED_HANDLES`, and the words of the new refusals in English and Korean.

Its picture is saved from the persona on its settings, named for what it is: its profile picture, as a PNG.

- [`36fe236`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/36fe236e4e793a0964a4c3aa62e19e257e7ce15c) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account has a persona the console keeps, and an avatar the package draws (ADR-0084). `GET /api/v1/agents/proposals?count=&lang=` answers a few personas to choose from, a name in the language asked for, a handle nobody has and the parts of an avatar, each from a seed; `POST /api/v1/agents` rolls the parts where none is sent; `PATCH /api/v1/agents/<handle>` keeps the name, the title and the avatar, for a supervisor. The avatar is its parts and nothing else (ADR-0097): a small planet, its body, what lies on its surface, the ramp of colours both are drawn in and one thing beside it or nothing, on a ground, with the seed the faint squares on the ground are strewn by. `avatarPixels` draws it as pixels, the same on every machine, `avatarDataUrl` as the address of a picture a page shows at once, `avatarScanlines` and `pngFile` as a PNG file up to 1024 pixels a side, with no renderer but the package's own; `rollAvatar` gives the parts of a seed; `AVATAR_SET_1` is the first set, four bodies with ten surfaces in eighteen ramps of colours and five things to stand beside a body, which never changes. A set is data (`AvatarSet`): grounds and ramps as colours, bodies, surfaces and accents as outlines in a square of a hundred units, each shape in two tones, the lighter left of a line and the darker right; the package fills them by how much of each pixel they cover. A picture under 96 pixels a side has no squares on its ground, and one under 64 has its planet drawn closer, up to 1.1 times (`AVATAR_PLAIN_UNDER`, `AVATAR_CLOSE_UNDER`, `AVATAR_CLOSE_MOST`); a set may say how much larger than written its planet is drawn at every size (`fill`, up to `AVATAR_FILL_MOST`), which the first set does by a twentieth (ADR-0099). The `Avatar` component draws an agent account's picture wherever a member is shown, as a soft square where a person's is round (`AvatarPicture`, and `AvatarSetsContext` for a console that brings a set of its own through `createConsole`'s `persona.avatarSets`). `console agents` lists the agent accounts, `console agent <handle>` reads one, and `console agent <handle> --avatar <file>` writes its avatar as a PNG, for its profile elsewhere. The names proposed are in the language packs (`persona.names`), a spelling per language; `proposeAgents` and `PERSONA_NAMES` for whoever proposes from them.

- [`9f33d5c`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/9f33d5c063749259d58650200c772f198aa44c6e) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account keeps its secrets in the console (ADR-0087): named values, sealed with the deployment's key for their account and their name, which the account alone reads back. The account, signed in as itself, has `console secrets` (the names), `console secret <name>` (the value, and nothing else), `console secret <name> --set` (typed unseen, or from `--file`, with `--expires` for a value that ends on a known day) and `--remove`; a token the account made for an agent of its own reads none of them. A supervisor has `console agent secrets <handle>` and `console agent secret <handle> <name> [--remove]`: a secret seeded write-only, so that a person or an agent above hands the account a credential it cannot make for itself, and never read back. Every setting, reading and removal is on the account's record, by name, read by its supervisors (`GET /api/v1/agents/<handle>/secret-accesses`), and none is an event of the feed. A handle is none of the words that follow `console agent` in the command (`secret`, `tell`, `stop` and the like), beside the words of its addresses. For whoever builds a console: `restAgentSecretSchema` and its kind, `isSecretName`, `SECRET_ACCESSES`, the bounds `MAX_AGENT_SECRET_NAME_LENGTH`, `MAX_AGENT_SECRET_BYTES` and `MAX_AGENT_SECRETS`, and `ConsoleClient.secrets`, `secret`, `setSecret`, `removeSecret`, `agentSecrets`, `seedAgentSecret`, `removeAgentSecret` and `agentSecretAccesses`.

- [`0683d8f`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/0683d8f2b4e661cbf11ea7e940016e324864bd27) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account's page reads as a conversation, where it read as a log. What the agent was told stands at the right, with who said it and through what, the pictures that came with it and the other files to save; what the agent said at the left, beside its picture; a thought is folded to its first line; a tool's call, what it answered and what was asked before it ran are one row that opens, a failed one marked; a wake, a status and an error are notes between. The box to speak in takes files of any kind, chosen, pasted or dropped on it, a picture shown small in its chip; Enter tells and Shift with Enter is a new line, never while a word is still being composed and not on a screen that is touched; it says before sending where the agent's product takes no picture, and the message says after where the product was not shown one. A stop, a restart and a reset are apart from the box, under Controls, each with words for the agent and with what it does. The page's views are the console's own sub-tabs, in the order Live, Work, Sessions, Settings, and are offered only to whoever may open them; the login the account signs in with is among its settings; the effort is said in Korean.

- [`b472c89`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b472c89f6077de39e2347c4a7fea8f7e8eb3951e) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - An agent account no runner has spoken for says so in a badge, "Not set up", as the others say how they stand. Its sessions are rows on shared columns that each lead to the session, with an end time read against the day the session began, and its settings stand in two columns where there is room. `Time` takes `precision="clock"`, which gives the hour alone for a moment on the day it is read against and the day with the hour for another, and `against`, the moment whose day that is: now, where none is given.

- [`b99e04d`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b99e04d04c49749554125cda5d59c41a9d865382) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A runner that takes an update of its command (ADR-0101) no longer ends inside the turn the update was made in. The message by which a runner the console refuses as too old tells its agent to install the console's version is a turn as any other, so the runner ends when the agent is done with it and never while the install still runs under it; the second sighting of the new version counts a heartbeat's interval after the first, where two looks a moment apart, at a change of the agent's state, were taken as two. A supervisor's `interrupt` stops the turn under way once an update was seen, where it stopped nothing until the turn ended by itself; its words reach the runner started after. The install line the agent is told names the console's version only where that is a version, and `latest` otherwise.

- [`48c72dc`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/48c72dc45c721629ce0044c175ef62667a97dceb) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console runner` saves the desk at the end of every session (ADR-0088): where the desk is a git repository and `runner.json` leaves `autosave` on, everything the desk does not ignore is committed, as "Autosave at the end of a session", where anything changed, and pushed, a net under the agent's own commits. A step git refuses is said in the runner's line by how git ended, and the runner goes on. For whoever runs a runner of their own: `createRunner` takes `git`, and `AUTOSAVE_MESSAGE` is the commit's words.

- [`5c0a9de`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/5c0a9ded8fe1e18859b17fd0ea25a164c0c2f4d1) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The `claude` driver lists Claude Code 2.1.322 as a version it was tried with: a word sent while a tool ran, taken when the call ended, and an interrupt with no words were run with it under the runner.

- [`2806290`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/2806290dbebcda874aee4ffa9f58dd2d42a9aae3) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console agent setup --console <origin> --code <code>` makes a machine an agent account's (ADR-0088), from the line of its invitation, in the agent's OS account and with no administrator's rights. After the guide it signs the command in as the account: the page opens in the machine's browser, the account says there that the command is it, and a sign-in that turns out to be anyone else's is given up with nothing set up. It then settles the desk, which is left as it is where there is one, cloned from the git host where the account pushed one before, with its own `git.token` handed to git in the environment, or written from the template over no file that is there; writes the service that keeps the runner running, `launchd` on macOS, and loads it; and says whether the runner is heard from, with the machine and the OS account, which the account's page shows beside the session. `--guide` prints the guide alone, `--desk <dir>` names the desk, `--new-desk` begins a new one, `--no-service` writes no service, and `--refresh` derives the machine again when the services or the account changed. The `launchd` service is written from the system's documentation and was not run on a Mac yet. For whoever builds a console: `launchd`, `launchdUnit` and `LAUNCHD_LABEL` in `@skillcdn/console/runner`, the `Service` interface with its `ServiceInput` and `ServicePlan`, `deskRemoteOf`, and `deskRemote` on a `ServiceProvider`.

- [`a7cf050`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a7cf05071fd0b09e3d588da9f1c1681830641964) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console runner` (ADR-0085): the runner of an agent account, started in its desk on the command signed in as the account itself. It starts the agent's product as a child process, hands it what the console sends as messages under a header that carries a mark only the session knows, with what came from outside the console in a fenced block; holds what arrives together and delivers it as one message; keeps one session at a time and reports everything the product does as the session's transcript, in numbered batches, with every value of the account's secret store taken out first; and says every thirty seconds that it is there. It restarts nothing by itself: an agent the model refused, or whose product failed, waits, a supervisor's word is tried at once and what arrives by itself after a cool-down that doubles. A supervisor's `stop` holds until `restart`, which goes on with the conversation, or `reset`, which begins a new one. `runner.json` in the desk names the driver and the secrets a session is handed, as environment variables and as files under `work/`. The first driver is `claude`, over the product's streaming JSON input and output. The heartbeat's answer carries `delivered`. For whoever builds on it: the entry point `@skillcdn/console/runner` (`createRunner`, the `Driver` types, `createRunnerModules`, `composeSignals`, `redactorOf`, `parseRunnerConfig`), and `ConsoleClient.signalStream`.

They kept to a reading width alone, which left the right of the page empty under a week drawn across it. `.sc-work-lists` lays them out; a custom console that set its `max-width` keeps it.

- [`8d71931`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8d719317c0f42ed95d81d1d7e563bf2ba1e26c89) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A supervisor sends pictures with a `tell` or an `interrupt` (ADR-0086): `ConsoleClient.sendSignal(handle, input, pictures)` sends them as the parts of a form, up to `MAX_SIGNAL_ATTACHMENTS`, and the signal's `attachments` name them. The runner reads them (`readSignalFile`) and hands them to the product as image blocks, and they are kept with what the agent was sent in the session's transcript; a supervisor's page shows them at `signalFileUrl`. Two refusals name the effort and the sign-in service in Korean, where they kept the record's own English words. The English pack is unchanged.

- [`085c5a2`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/085c5a2068df5eefcc79b8de2538fbec4a71c1f2) Thanks [@samo-mozzi](https://github.com/samo-mozzi)! - `console serve` passes on the referrer's origin when the page is on this machine (ADR-0083), as `Referer: <origin>/`, so that a plugin's widgets and pages show in a console a person serves from their machine, or in a development server in front of the command: the console reads that origin to know which page may embed a frame, and without it answered every frame with a 404. The path, query and fragment never travel, nor a referrer from anywhere else; cookies and `Origin` are stripped as before.

- [`bc2d70c`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/bc2d70cdd5a48554d12246cd4bbda207188df12c) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Signals and the heartbeat of an agent account's runner, on the console's side (ADR-0085). A supervisor sends an agent account a signal, in their own name and numbered on the account's record: `console agent tell | interrupt | stop | restart | reset | wake <handle>` with words for the agent, and `console agent set <handle> --model <name> --effort <level>` for what it runs with; `console agent signals <handle|me>` reads the record (`POST` and `GET /api/v1/agents/<handle>/signals`, `GET /api/v1/agents/me/signals`). The runner receives the signals on a stream from the number it names (`GET /api/v1/agents/me/signals/stream`) and says how its agent stands with a heartbeat (`PUT /api/v1/agents/me/runner`), both on the account's command alone. An agent carries `state`, `running`, `stopped`, `refused`, `failed` or `unheard` with why and since when, for whoever sees it, and `runner`, what its runner last said and what was chosen for it, for its supervisors and itself; `console agent <handle>` prints both. A `stop` holds until a `restart` or a `reset`, a runner not heard from for five minutes is noted `unheard`, and every change is `agent.state_changed` in the workspace's feed, delivered to a plugin with `people:read:all` as the people's events are. For whoever builds a console: `restSignalSchema`, `restSignalsSchema`, `restSignalInputSchema`, `restRunnerHeartbeatInputSchema`, `restRunnerHeartbeatSchema`, `restAgentStateSchema`, `restAgentRunnerSchema`, `SIGNAL_KINDS`, `SIGNAL_SOURCES`, `AGENT_STATE_REASONS`, `MAX_SIGNAL_LENGTH`, and `ConsoleClient.sendSignal`, `signals` and `runnerHeartbeat`.

- [`71c5bf3`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/71c5bf3623ed364bc0683d98b3a326f752e34b8b) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Four small things seen when each part was looked at by itself. A picker opened in a person's card shows its list over the card after it, where the next card was painted over the list. A quote in what people and agents wrote stands at the text's own edge, without the browser's margin beside its line, and an item of a task list shows its box where a bullet would stand, not both. On a phone a calendar's bar, which shows no pictures there, shows no count of the pictures left out either.

- [`8577423`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8577423306375a7599f81db0a1e5fdde3dc02371) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console agent setup` keeps a runner running on Linux too (ADR-0085): the `systemd-user` service, a unit of the agent's own OS account, `~/.config/systemd/user/skillcdn-console-runner.service`, turned on and started by that account's own systemd with no administrator's rights; its lines are in the account's journal. Setup asks for lingering for the account itself, so that the runner outlives the account's session and starts with the machine; where a system keeps that for an administrator, setup says what is left to do. `console agent setup --refresh` has the system read a unit it wrote again, which ends nothing that runs. Run on Ubuntu 24.04 with systemd 255: the unit loaded, the runner heard from, and started again after it ended. For whoever builds on the runner: `systemdUser`, `systemdUnit` and `SYSTEMD_UNIT` in `@skillcdn/console/runner`; a service's command may carry what it adds to the environment, what is left to do where it may fail and did, and how often it is tried, and a service may name the commands that have the system read its files again (`ServiceCommand`, `ServicePlan.reread`).

- [`f468fd8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/f468fd8ff6f966759f4de134027a7106296592b4) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console agent setup` keeps a runner running on Windows too (ADR-0085): the `task-scheduler` service, a scheduled task of the agent's own OS account, `SkillCDN Console Runner (<account>)`, registered by that account with no administrator's rights and started when it logs on, so that the runner runs while the account is logged on at the machine. The task starts the runner in the desk, in no window, with the `PATH` setup ran with, keeps its lines in `~\AppData\Local\skillcdn-console\runner.log`, and starts it again ten seconds after it ends; ending the task ends the runner, and one runner runs for the account. `console agent setup --refresh` registers a definition it wrote again, which ends nothing that runs. Run on Windows 11: the task registered and started, the runner heard from, a turn of Claude Code under it, the runner started again after it ended and ended with the task. `console runner` ends when the process that keeps it does, where its environment names that process (`CONSOLE_RUNNER_KEEPER`), which is how a task that the system ends by its one process takes the runner with it. For whoever builds on the runner: `taskScheduler`, `taskDefinition`, `taskScript`, `taskNameOf`, `TASK_NAME` and `KEEPER_VARIABLE` in `@skillcdn/console/runner`; what a service is told of the runner now names the OS account too (`ServiceInput.user`).

- [`469d43c`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/469d43c4c3a60ba7100f1c2288f1d6ee7059e101) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The way from an agent account added to one at work is worded again (ADR-0094). "Add an agent account" is in three parts, who it is, the account it signs in with, and whom it reports to and where it starts; under the login it says what kind of name it is, an address at Google Workspace or a login at GitHub, and that the account need not be there yet and has to be before its machine is set up. Nothing on a page, in the command, in the guide or in the onboarding task says who makes an account at a service or what a service's terms ask: that is the organization's to say. Until its runner is heard from, an account's page shows what is next in order, the account, an invitation, the one line on its machine, its runner, each said done as the console sees it, in the pages' own words in English and Korean, where the lines of a service's module stood in English. The setup's guide has the agent ask the person at the machine for the sign-in before the command goes on, in words it can say as they are, and has a part of its own on signing in elsewhere in a browser: a browser tool attached to the machine's ordinary browser, where a sign-in stays, a person signing in inside the agent's session where one is wanted, and nothing the agent signs in with in a message. `console agent new` says the login and when it has to be there. For whoever builds a console: `AgentStartSteps`; `AgentInvitationPanel` takes `intro` and no longer `first`; `SetupGuideSection.key` gains `browser`; the words `agents.accounts.form.first` are gone and `agents.page.start` is new.

- [`554bb49`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/554bb49d9c33e38f91f674bc2a3cd3d51cf7df20) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The runner drives any product that speaks the Agent Client Protocol (ADR-0085): the `acp` driver, with the command that speaks it named in the desk, `"driver": "acp", "product": { "command": "<program>", "args": [...] }` in `runner.json`. The runner is the protocol's client and offers no file and no terminal of its own, so that the agent works with its own tools; a conversation is resumed or loaded where the agent can; a message that arrives in a turn is the next turn's, and the turn is cancelled for one that is to be read now; what the agent asks leave for is answered at once, allowed once, or refused where `permissions` says `deny`; `permissions` may also name one of the agent's own modes, which the session is set to, with what it still asks refused, and an agent the driver was run against is set to the mode it has for a run nobody attends where the desk names none (ADR-0091); the model and the level of thought are the session's own choices, set in a running session. A tool call's result is what the call held last, what its command printed where the agent sends that beside the protocol, or its output as data; a conversation the agent cannot go on with is said and a new one begun; why a request was refused is read from the error's message and from what it carries beside it. What an agent tells beside its work is taken as a notice and recorded as a status, and a turn the agent ends where it was refused leave is said as that. Written from the protocol's documentation, version 1, read against the types the protocol publishes, and run against two agents, the adapters that put Codex (2.6.0) and Claude Code (0.106.0) behind the protocol, on Windows: the Agents page marks any other as a version the driver was not tried with. A tool call is said once what it was called with is known, since an agent may open one before its input is whole, and one opened under an id whose call is done is another call. What a session runs with is said again when a model or a level is taken in it, or changed by the agent, so that the agent's page says what it runs with now. `product.command` also names another binary for the `claude` and `codex` drivers. For whoever builds on the runner: `createAcpDriver` and `acpMode` in `@skillcdn/console/runner`, and `args` and `version` on what a driver is made with.

- [`c4a6364`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/c4a63646b48928c06d459ef429a5a3158b9d3b43) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - On an agent's page, a tell that is refused is said at the box to speak in, not above the session. The console's file picker (`sc-file-choice`) shows where the keyboard is on every form that has one. One session's page names its list once for a screen reader. The Korean pack names no product where the picture's hint names the messenger and the identity provider.

- [`807e3c8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/807e3c8b144736b51a24b9a5a49dc7428b8a0ebf) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account has a page of its own in the default UI (ADR-0085, ADR-0086), at `/agents/<handle>`: what it is doing as it happens, its supervisor saying something to it there with pictures, interrupting it, stopping and restarting it, resetting it, and choosing its model and effort from what its product offers; its sessions, each read whole; its plate, as a person's; and its settings, what its runner said of itself, where it is signed in, its secrets by name with the way to seed one, and its persona. Everything an agent's product printed is shown as text and never made markup. The list of agent accounts says how each stands and leads to its page (`AgentAccountList` takes `href`), and an agent that reports to a person and is not running is on that person's My work. `agentHref` and `agentWorkHref` name the addresses.

- [`8bf421b`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8bf421bb60a11e76f1f5b8e7a4edf1a35d8aae8c) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The Agents page lists agent accounts (ADR-0084): each with its avatar, its name and what it does, its handle, whom it reports to, "Invited" until its first sign-in, and for its supervisors its picture to save as a PNG for its profile elsewhere, drawn in the browser. An administrator adds one at `/agents/new/account`: a few personas proposed and rolled again, the name, the handle and the title edited, the login it will sign in with, proposed from the handle and what the deployment's provider admits and edited freely, its manager, themselves unless another, and the project it starts in. `console agent new <handle> --login <login>` makes one with flags. A console that holds a token shows the agent accounts on its Agents page, and nothing of the tokens, as before. For whoever builds a console: `AgentAccountList`, `AgentAccountForm`, `suggestLogin`, `avatarPngBlob`, `ConsoleComponents.AgentAccountList` and `AgentAccountForm` to replace them, `ConsoleData.agents`, `createAgent` and `agentProposals` among the actions, `PATHS.newAgent`, and `createConsole`'s `persona.names`, which the form proposes from in place of the console's own.

- [`4507c38`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/4507c3887df4a40efcfd4a89aaefeacdca8e3035) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The Agents tab is in views, as the People tab and the settings are: the agent accounts that report to whoever looks, directly or through someone who does, at `/agents`, with every other agent account of the workspace under a heading of its own there for an administrator; and the agents that act as the person, each with a token of theirs, at `/agents?view=connected`, with the token made by hand and the way to connect one. An agent account's card says how it stands, what it is doing now and since when, and what it runs, and names its manager where that is someone else; the login it signs in with is said on its page. The People tab lists the people and then the agent accounts, each under a heading with how many, and an account's name and a way at the foot of its card lead to its page. For whoever builds a console: `agentsHref`, `AGENTS_VIEWS` and `AgentsView`; the route `agents` carries its `view`; `AgentAccountList` takes `me`; `PeopleList` takes `agentHref`.

- [`bc7b529`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/bc7b529b44116eb70664ba9a8a68baefa2076349) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The Agents tab's views are named for what they hold: "Agent accounts you manage", since a manager owns nothing, and "Connected agents". The view that listed every account for an administrator is gone (`?view=all`, `AGENTS_VIEWS` without `all`): an administrator finds the accounts someone else manages under their own, beneath a heading. For whoever builds a console: `agentAccountGroups(agents, me, people)` says which accounts are managed and which are the others; the words `agents.views.all` and `agents.accounts.allIntro` are gone, and `agents.accounts.others` and `othersIntro` are new.

- [`d465644`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d46564407ff9d758ec6d2acf8cb9e2355b350b46) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The autosave of `console runner` pushes with the account's own token. The push at the end of a session is handed the token the account's store keeps as `git.token`, where it keeps one and the workspace names a git host: answered by a credential helper in the environment of that one command, on no command line and in no file. An account that kept its token in the store alone, as its desk tells it to, ended every session with "committed and not pushed". The helper, here and for the clone `console agent setup` makes, is named for the remote's own origin, so that a redirect or a repository at another host is answered nothing of the token. The autosave stages nothing under the desk's `work/`, by name, whatever the desk's `.gitignore` says: what a supervisor sent stays on the machine. The lines setup prints for a person to start the runner by hand quote the desk and the login where they hold anything a shell reads as more than a word. `RunnerOptions.git` is handed `{ token }` for a step that reaches the git host, and `GIT_TOKEN_SECRET` names the secret.

- [`7293224`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/7293224bb79646944596ef16e7f472da6beaa623) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The board wakes agent accounts by its rules (ADR-0085): a ready task an agent is at work on whose day is unset, today or past, an agent made a worker of one, a due day moved to today, a comment on a task it owns or works on, a decision addressed to it and the answer to one it raised, and, from the console, a change of the services the workspace names. The worker role reads the feed for them every few seconds; what an agent did itself wakes it for nothing, and a task planned for a later day wakes nobody. A `task.updated` event that changed the workers says whom the change put to work, `workersAdded`, which the feed's schema reads.

- [`2dbe6d8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/2dbe6d8bc4d1a8c280955bc76dc76ac341416610) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - An agent account starts its day with a briefing (ADR-0085). The console sends it a wake at the hours it keeps, in the workspace's time zone: at the day's start, 09:00 where the account says no other hour, a few lines: its tasks planned for today and those carried over, the first few by name, how many are open with no day and how many decisions wait; on the week's first day, the week's, in its place; at the day's end, where it has one, a wrap, to write the day down and push its desk. The hours are kept by the account or a supervisor: `console agent <handle> --day-start 08:30 --day-end 18:00`, `none` for the usual, "Its day" on the account's Settings, `dayStart` and `dayEnd` on `PATCH /api/v1/agents/<handle>`, and `hours` where one account is read. A month before a secret's day the console tells the account, by the secret's name and never its value. For whoever builds a console: `restAgentHoursSchema` and `DEFAULT_DAY_START`.

- [`b050303`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b0503037d5f4a2c3c7fa5caca2367bd7d551c2b0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The `claude` driver was run against the product's binary, 2.1.321, and corrected to what it showed (ADR-0085). An `interrupt` stops the tool call under way at once: the product's own `now` waits for the call to finish, so the driver first sends the request that stops a turn, the one the product's client library sends. The runner knows when an agent is done with what it was sent: the product says each message back when it takes it (`--replay-user-messages`), and a turn is completed only when none still waits, so that a message written as a turn was ending, or the one a turn was stopped for, is not lost to a session that ends in between. "The product started." is in a transcript once and not at every turn, with the permission mode the product runs in, and says so where that is not the mode asked for; a turn that was stopped is a status and no error; a denial is said once; `manual` is a mode `runner.json` may name; the effort an account chose stays said where the product names none. The version is listed as tried, so the account's page no longer says otherwise of it. For whoever writes a driver: `DriverHost.uuid`, the ids a driver writes its messages under.

An agent account is named by its handle wherever the command takes a login (`--worker @mira`; `GET /api/v1/people?login=@mira`). A console served through the command's session offers nothing a token cannot do, as one served through a token. For whoever builds a console: `ConnectApproval` asks "This is me" of a connection whose `as` is `self`, `SessionList` names a command's session, `restManagerSchema`, `SESSION_KINDS`, and `ConsoleClient.manager`, `agentSignIns` and `endAgentSignIn`.

The pane takes a scroll for the reader's own only after a wheel, a touch, a key or a press on it, so that it goes on following its end when the conversation changes how tall it is by itself; and a session is read once, when it is first shown, where the page read it again from its start every time it asked how the session stood and the count of its events had grown, which emptied the conversation under whoever was reading it. `followsAfter` takes whether the scroll was by hand in the place of where the pane stood, and `HAND_MS` is how long after a hand's touch a scroll counts as one.

The kinds the package knows, `github`, `google-workspace` and `slack`, say what a person does there, what the agent does when it starts and what is asked of a person; another kind is kept as it came. For whoever builds a console: `agentLoginOf`, `serviceAccountOf`, `describeService`, `serviceProviderOf`, `fillTemplate`, `SERVICE_PROVIDERS`, `SERVICE_ROLES`, `serviceProblem`, `isDomain`, `restWorkspaceServicesSchema`, `WorkspaceServicesView` and `WorkspaceServicesForm`, and `settingsHref("services")`.

- [`b08aee8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/b08aee8573ac996c6b983893a071e1b3dca216ec) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - What the review before 0.5.0 found of who an account is, on the console's side. `GET /api/v1/me` leaves the workspace's `handle`, `domain` and `services` out for nobody signed in, who was told what the organization's accounts are named from; each is read as optional, and the sign-in page reads none. A session is noted as used only for a member who is in: a runner that went on asking while its account was out carried the command's session past the day it would have ended, and was the account again the moment it was taken back in; out for longer than a session lasts unused, the account now signs its command in again. The command signed in as the account itself is taken at a plugin's endpoint as a token is, where a change by it counted for nobody. The access log masks an invitation's code in a path as it masks a connect code. A connect code drawn twice is drawn again, where the retry never ran and the answer was `500`. And an error is logged with its name, its codes, its statement and where it was thrown from, never with what the statement was given: a failed query carried every value bound to it into the log, the words of a comment or of an agent's conversation among them.

## 0.4.7

### Patch Changes

- [`1d57b33`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/1d57b33815b8ddd2fd703fbcbe28a8e02af17312) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A group tab in the header opens its list of plugins over the page: the list was clipped by the row of tabs, which scrolls sideways, and could be neither seen nor pressed wherever a group held two plugins. The list is a popover in the top layer, placed under its tab with the names lined up under the tab's and within the window, measured as it opens, dismissed by a click elsewhere or the escape key, its caret turned while it is open; it names each plugin alone, the one the page is on in the accent. A mouse resting on the tab opens the list, and leaving the tab and the list closes it; a mouse's click on the tab goes where the tab leads, the group's first page; a finger, a pen or a key opens and closes the list by a press. The person's menu lists a group's plugins one by one under the group's name, where it led to the first alone. A list, or the person's menu, longer than the window has room for scrolls within itself, so that its last items, Sign out among them, are always reached; both close when the window loses the focus, as it does to a click in a plugin's frame, which the page never sees; and where the browser has no popover (Safari before 17) the tab shows and hides the list itself. A tab's name wider than the row has room for stops with an ellipsis, and the tab's title says it whole, Hangul counted as running about twice as wide. The menus are drawn on the raised surface, with a shadow of their own (`--sc-shadow-menu`, in `CONSOLE_TOKENS`).

- [`1ca178c`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/1ca178c00574f25a7fc8c58ec7fd7f5ae2f18550) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin's frame has a spinner in its place until the plugin's page has drawn, on the page itself, in a widget on the front page, and when a plugin's sub-tab is pressed: until the page grows past the height it had before it drew anything, or five seconds after the frame loaded or said `ready`, whichever is first. My work and My decisions, come back to, stand at once with what they showed last and read again behind it, rather than wait on the server every time their tab is pressed; what they showed is forgotten when the person signs out. The workspace's lists and the person's tokens are asked for along with who is signed in, rather than after: one round trip fewer before any page stands.

- [`8c08236`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8c08236283077bdb4d4cd5cdf5477a5233c3edde) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console plugin <key> secret <name>`, `console login --token-stdin` and the `pack-token --stdin` commands ask for the value at the terminal and take it typed unseen, where a value typed there was echoed on the screen before; what a key that types nothing sends, an arrow, Delete, Home, or the marks a terminal puts around a paste, is not taken into the value; piped in or redirected from a file, standard input is read as before. The help, the specs and the skill `planning-the-board` say never to give a secret on the command line, through `echo` or through an agent. The Korean pages say a token is kept encrypted, where they said sealed.

- [`8ea14c8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/8ea14c842725885bfcb3414194e72646cb817765) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A workspace has a language for whoever has none of their own (ADR-0081): an administrator sets one the console speaks on the Settings page or with `console workspace edit --language <en|ko|none>` (`PATCH /api/v1/workspace`, `language`), and `GET /api/v1/me` answers it. `ctx.console.workspace()` answers `language`, the workspace's or `en`, and `languageSet`, with `languages` putting it first, and the frame's `init.workspace` carries `language`; a call for a person with no language, and the console's own hooks, jobs, enabling, disabling and upgrades, run in it, and the pages fall back on it after the browser's languages. `chooseLanguage` takes it as a fourth argument; `languagesFirst` and the type `ConsoleLanguage` are exported.

- [`800b0ae`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/800b0ae88e97165881e88bf5abb8468b9e1813ab) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console-plugin check` no longer reads `ctx.fetch(`, or any member called `fetch`, private ones (`this.#fetch(`) included, as a call of the global `fetch` the host removes, which failed every server bundle that used the context's fetch as the spec asks; `globalThis.fetch(` is still found, and `global.fetch(` and `globalThis?.fetch(` now are. `defineEndpoint` takes a webhook's `secret` named as settings are named (`slackSigningSecret`), where it took the shape of a plugin key, which no setting has.

- [`46f9bf9`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/46f9bf9749b1deacbcb55e7ac02f4bdd9fa16d40) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A person sees the browsers they are signed in on, under Settings, in a view of their own beside the workspace's (`/settings/sessions`): each as a browser on a platform, when it was last used, where it last came from and the day it signed in, the current one marked; and signs any other out, or all the others at once. `GET /api/v1/sessions`, `DELETE /api/v1/sessions/<id>` and `DELETE /api/v1/sessions/others`, for a person on the console's own pages and never for a token; `SessionList` is a component of the package, the client has `sessions`, `endSession` and `endOtherSessions`, and the composition's actions read the sessions when the view opens. A session whose time ended is not found when it is signed out, as one ended already is, and the view reads the sessions again whether a sign-out went or not. A session begun before this names no browser.

## 0.4.6

### Patch Changes

- [`c6a894a`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/c6a894ae8de9633e44fb65a3245c9c7bc3fdb00f) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin's frame opens its links itself (ADR-0078): `frame.open(url)` opens a web or a mail address in a new tab from inside the frame, whose sandbox now lets a window out of it, and the browser holds the frame to a press in it, as it holds any page that opens a window: no window on the frame's own time or for a press elsewhere on the page, and the call is refused with no recent press where the browser can say so. The console's page opened the link before, for any recent press on the page, which a frame that took the focus could have had for itself. `frame.open` needs a console from 0.4.6, which a plugin that calls it says in its manifest's `console`; for a bundle built before, the page still opens a link as far as it can tell of a press in the frame, until the next line of SkillCDN.

- [`d81fadb`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/d81fadb0ec40c0e1b832f1f436586f265a29e69b) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A person's own decision is its project's (ADR-0077): once taken out of a private project, the people it is addressed to and the person who raised it no longer read it at its address, read its files or answer it, nor find it among their decisions, on their plate or in the count beside My decisions, where they went on doing all of it; listed again, they have it back. A plugin's decision reaches past its project as before, to those it is addressed to, who raised it and the managers above them. `ctx.console.decisions.answer` for a person answers a person's own decision only while they are in its project.

- [`a11e320`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a11e320a12224523d3817089db7616719de004d8) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: a plugin reaches its board project and the projects it is placed on, and no other (ADR-0080). A person's call took it to the project the call was on, wherever an administrator had placed it; `ctx.console.decisions.ask`, `tasks.create` and the other calls on such a project are `plugin.forbidden` now, until the plugin is placed on it. Whoever may use a plugin is a requester in its board project: a person's call, or a plugin's call for a person, raises a decision there with `ask` whether the person may see the project or not, and the decision reaches them as its raiser, at its own address and among the decisions they raised, nothing else of the project opening to them; before, the call was `plugin.not_found`, and every requester had to be listed in the project, where they read and answered everyone's. `decisions.get` and `runs.get` for a person answer only what the person may see, as a listing does. The model of who may do what through a plugin is stated whole in the spec's "Security model".

- [`869e2a1`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/869e2a1cfc19436015e38e1db67aa9a27f1baf14) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `ctx.fetch` takes a secret out of the answer in more of the encodings a service sends one back in (ADR-0079): percent-encoding with small hex digits, JSON that escapes its slashes, and base64 in a header's alphabet or a URL's, whole or inside a longer run of it, for every placement and not only `basic`, a value of 8 bytes or more.

- [`985f718`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/985f7187b4f2010e2980a276c9f7cae6098c896b) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `console file <id> --decision <id>` reads a file put on a decision at the decision's own address, wherever the decision is, and `console decision` and `console answer` name that command for each file of a decision reached outside the project the command works in, where the `console file <id>` they named was not found. The client reads one with `readDecisionFile(decisionId, fileId)`. `console help` lists `console teams` and `console team`, which it left out.

## 0.4.5

### Patch Changes

- [`376e282`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/376e2822430b1a9f7a2d7cffc4c1b50a329e8613) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A collection may forget: `defineCollection(name, { ..., retention: { days, by? } })` keeps a record `days` (1 to `MAX_PLUGIN_RETENTION_DAYS`, 3650) after the moment `by` names, `createdAt` unless said, `updatedAt`, or an indexed field holding a day or an instant as ISO 8601 text; every hour the worker removes what is past it, archived or not, with its versions, links and files, so that a collection of deliveries or log lines never reaches the plugin's quota, which counts a record while it is kept. With `history: false` it is a light collection for logs. `CollectionDefinition` and `CollectionDescription` carry `retention` (`null` for as long as the plugin is), `GET /api/v1/plugins/<key>` answers `keeps`, the collections forgotten after a while with their days (read as optional), and the plugin's settings page says what it keeps and for how long.

- [`08e1c2e`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/08e1c2e6eeda8ec006a8a5d3bab3a8f0f7d3b810) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - `arg.day` and a `day` setting take a day the calendar has. February 30 and April 31 fit the shape `YYYY-MM-DD` and reached the plugin, where a record's schema refused them in the record's words and a query answered as if they were days; both are now `plugin.invalid_args`, named as the argument. `isCalendarDay` is exported from `@skillcdn/console/plugin`.

- [`2dd1993`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/2dd19937ff0b3ee4b3340a952c050f2d1c8d1679) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A decision reaches whom it is addressed to wherever it is ([ADR-0068](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0068-a-decision-reaches-whom-it-is-addressed-to-wherever-it-is-and-a-plugins-reaches-the-managers-above-them.md)). A person finds a decision addressed to them among their decisions and on their plate in a project they may see or not, reads it at `GET /api/v1/decisions/<id>` (`{ project, decision, mayAnswer }`, with `?wait=` as the project's route has it) and answers it at `POST /api/v1/decisions/<id>/answer`; the pages show one at `/decisions/<id>`, and My decisions leads there for a project the person may not see. A plugin's decision may address anyone active of the workspace, not only who may work in its board project, and reaches whoever manages a decider, up the reporting line, to read and answer; a person's own decision still names people of the project. `console decision <id>` and `console answer <id>` read the project the command works in first and then the decision wherever it is, so that an agent waits for a plugin's decision from any checkout, and a plugin command that raised one says the project it waits in (`project` on the invocation, read as optional). The client gains `decision`, `awaitDecision` and `answerDecision` at the workspace, `createConsole` the page, and a console built on the package reads an older console as before. A role of a plugin may follow the reporting line: `follows: "reports"` on a role in the manifest, held by whoever someone active reports to, granted by nobody; `declaredRoles` answers `follows` (read as optional), `ctx.console.people.granted(role)` lists them, and the settings page says so.

- [`067609b`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/067609b0dc9d004481459464db3e79e0a3784026) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A decision's files reach whom the decision reaches ([ADR-0068](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0068-a-decision-reaches-whom-it-is-addressed-to-wherever-it-is-and-a-plugins-reaches-the-managers-above-them.md)). The person a decision is addressed to outside its project, and for a plugin's decision a manager up the reporting line, opened it among their decisions and at `/decisions/<id>`, and its attachments and the pictures its context names answered `404` at the project's address for files. They are read at the decision's own address now, `GET /api/v1/decisions/<id>/files/<file id>`, for whoever the decision reaches, with a session or a token, served as the project's files are, and only the files of that decision; another file of the project and a decision out of reach are `file.not_found` alike. The client gains `decisionFileUrl(decisionId, fileId)`, and the routes `decisionFilePath`. `MyDecisions` reads a decision's files there where `fileHref` is left out, where it named them without a link, and gives a `fileHref` of a console's own the decision's id as a third argument; the page at `/decisions/<id>` reads them there too. A plugin command that raised a decision in a project other than its board says that project as where the decision waits (`project` on the invocation), and no project where the caller does not reach the decision.

- [`4a83344`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/4a833445f245eb769d2cfa2b1a372ab6b8a0a886) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The dispatcher delivers to each plugin under an advisory lock of its own, where one lock held every plugin in turn: two processes may dispatch to two plugins at once, and a process delivers to a few side by side, as many as its database connections allow. A plugin that has had twenty seconds of a pass leaves the rest of its events to the next pass, so that a plugin whose hook waits on a slow service, or waits out its backoff after a failure, holds up the others for no longer than that. A hook told to come back later by the service it called says so with `retryAfterMs` in the data of its refusal (`throw new PluginError("rate_limited", message, { retryAfterMs })`), and the next try waits that long, within an hour, in place of the doubling backoff; the try still counts toward the five.

- [`87a82b1`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/87a82b19479ae3e066f13291f5b377618b0a9d85) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: a hook's event carries `id`, one id per event across the console's events and the plugins' (`console:<number>`, `plugin:<number>`), and `attempt`, which delivery this is, 1 the first time. Delivery is at least once, and a hook that must not act twice kept a key of the kind, the record and the time, which two events of one second could share; it keeps the ids it acted on now. `PluginHookEvent` gains the two fields, so a test of a plugin's that builds an event by hand adds them.

- [`4e84625`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/4e846250bc01ff899308087b00b5e288537ec5cd) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin's job keeps its lock for as long as it runs, up to its five minutes, where a job of more than thirty seconds lost it to the server's bound on an idle transaction: another process could run the same job beside it, and the pass logged that the plugins' jobs were not run, leaving the jobs after it to the next pass.

- [`87a82b1`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/87a82b19479ae3e066f13291f5b377618b0a9d85) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A link a person presses in a plugin's frame opens in a new tab ([ADR-0067](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0067-a-link-a-person-presses-in-a-frame-opens-in-a-new-tab-through-the-bridge.md)). `frame.open(url)` in `@skillcdn/console/plugin/web` sends `open` on the bridge, and the console's page opens a web or a mail address (`https:`, `http:`, `mailto:`) with no opener and no referrer, only right after a person pressed; anything else is refused on both sides, and a path of the console's stays `navigate`'s. The frame's sandbox is unchanged, and no capability is asked. A frame could open nothing before, and copied the address for the person to paste.

- [`c90f465`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/c90f465e4c64b983d0b501581dc72bb1a8b45727) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: a plugin calls no api of its own. A manifest that asks `plugins:<its own key>`, required or optional, is refused (`capabilities: plugins:<key> is the plugin itself`), and `ctx.plugins.get(<its own key>)` is `plugin.forbidden` whatever its install holds: through its api a plugin was called as another plugin, and with `people:act` it could make every call of its own for anyone (ADR-0072). Its code runs its own functions directly.

- [`64117b1`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/64117b13fb01a11e03bfb86bc740edcd27e973c0) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: a plugin that calls another plugin from its own job, hook or webhook no longer runs the callee with every role; the callee sees `{ kind: "plugin", plugin, roles, for: null }`, the calling plugin with the roles an administrator granted to it and the callee's default role (ADR-0072). A role may be granted to a plugin (`POST .../grants` with `plugin`, `console plugin <key> grant <role> --plugin <key>`, the settings page; `RestPluginGrant.plugin`, read as optional); a manifest asks for them with `grants: { <key>: { roles, why } }`, offered when enabling and listed on the plugin's page, answered as `asks` on `GET /api/v1/plugins/<key>`, and read by the plugin as `ctx.plugin.grants`. With the capability `people:act` a plugin acts for a person who did something elsewhere: `ctx.plugins.get(key).for(personId)` calls the other plugin with the roles both hold, in the person's language, and `ctx.console.decisions.answer(id, { optionKey | option, note? }, { for })` answers a decision by the person's own standing; the event names the person, the plugin and, as `via` (read as optional), the plugin that acted for them, and the feed says "through <plugin>". `PluginCaller` gains the kind `plugin` (`PluginAsCaller`), `createTestContext` takes `plugins` with `for`, and a plugin with `plugins:<key>` hears that plugin's `plugin.enabled`, `plugin.disabled` and `plugin.upgraded` in its hooks.

- [`55de3d8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/55de3d8fed3536d2150928ac7bf7a0f0e5515a94) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - Breaking: a secret no longer comes back to a plugin in an answer (ADR-0070, amended by ADR-0075). Sealing fails closed: where `seal` names values, an answer that is no JSON object or array reaches the plugin without its body, where it reached it whole before, a token endpoint's form with the token in it; a value that is no string, is over 4,096 characters or holds a secret the request carried from another setting is never sealed and reads `"[withheld]"`; and the answer carries `sealed`, what became of each value named (`sealed`, `absent`, `not_a_string`, `too_long`, `echoed`, `not_json`; `PluginFetchResponse.sealed`, `SealOutcome`), so that a plugin tells a token sealed from one it never received. A secret pinned to `hosts` is sealed from their answers alone, `plugin.forbidden` before the request goes out otherwise, so that another host cannot put a token or a signing secret of its own in its place. `ctx.fetch` takes `GET`, `HEAD`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE` and `OPTIONS` alone, `TRACE` and any other being `plugin.invalid_args`; and every form of a secret the console put into a request, as placed, escaped as JSON, URL-encoded, and for `basic` the base64 of the pair and the password, is taken out of the answer's headers and body, as is a value sealed or withheld wherever else the answer holds it, so that a server that echoes the request, or a redirect whose `location` carries a `query:` secret, hands the plugin `"[withheld]"`.

- [`35516c8`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/35516c8b38012253b3655cd0350c734de78366eb) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A secret is sealed by the console from an answer the plugin received, and goes where its setting says (ADR-0070). `ctx.fetch` takes `seal: { <setting>: "$.path" | { path, expiresIn } }`, values of the answer's JSON the console seals as the plugin's `secret` settings before the plugin reads the answer, which says `"[sealed]"` in their place, with their expiry; a request carries `secret` or `secrets`, five at most, each `as` `header:<name>`, `bearer`, `query:<name>`, `basic` or `body:<field>`. A `secret` setting may name `hosts`, the hosts it goes to and no other, and `use: "verify"`, one that verifies signatures and signs nothing. `ctx.secrets.has` is false past the expiry, and `ctx.secrets.status(name)` answers `{ set, setAt, expiresAt, source }`, `source` being `administrator` or the host the value was sealed from; `createTestContext` answers it too. A setting on `GET /api/v1/plugins/<key>` carries `expiresAt`, `source`, `hosts` and `use` (read as optional), the plugin's settings page and `console plugin <key> settings` say from where and until when, and the feed says a secret was sealed from a host's answer (`plugin.configured` with `source`).

- [`08e1c2e`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/08e1c2e6eeda8ec006a8a5d3bab3a8f0f7d3b810) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A time zone nobody set is told. `ctx.console.workspace()` says `timeZoneSet`, whether an administrator set one, beside `timeZone`, which is `UTC` either way, so that a plugin that reads a time of day says so instead of taking UTC for a choice. Where a plugin has a job at a time of day or a `time` setting and the workspace has no zone, `GET /api/v1/plugins/<key>` answers `needsTimeZone` (read as optional), the plugin's settings page shows the notice with the way to set one, and `console plugin <key> enable` prints it. A job's next time follows the zone: when it is set or changed, the next run is computed again at the job's time of day in the new zone, where the first run after the change was at the old zone's time; and a time of day across a clock change is read at the zone's offset on the day itself.

- [`a851776`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/a851776c216d2df4c8a7a91ed9bfc6e24eb2b80c) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - The directory keeps a work email ([ADR-0069](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/blob/main/docs/adr/0069-the-directory-keeps-a-work-email-read-by-an-administrator-the-person-and-a-plugin-allowed-to.md)). A person who signs in at Google has the address Google vouched for, written again at each sign-in; an administrator writes everyone else's, on the People page or with `console person email <login> <address|none>` (`PATCH /api/v1/people/<id>` with `email`, lowercase, one per workspace, `person.email_taken` otherwise). A person carries `email` (read as optional) to an administrator and to themselves alone, on `GET /api/v1/people` and `GET /api/v1/me`; everywhere else a person is named without it. A plugin reads it with the new capability `people:read:email`, "the work email of the people it may read, to match them to accounts elsewhere", on `ctx.console.people.list` and `get`; `console people` prints it in angle brackets where the console answers one. The event `person.email_changed` carries the login and whether an address is set, never the address, and reaches the hooks of a plugin that reads the directory. `PeopleList` takes `onChangeEmail`.

- [`c90f465`](https://github.com/skillcdn/console/commit/c90f465e4c64b983d0b501581dc72bb1a8b45727) Thanks [@samo-lucid](https://github.com/samo-lucid)! - A plugin acting for a person acts with the person's standing as a member, never their administration (ADR-0072, amended by ADR-0076). `ctx.console.decisions.answer(id, answer, { for })` answers only a decision on the person's own plate, addressed to them or a plugin's addressed to someone they manage, up the reporting line, and is `plugin.forbidden` for any other, one they could answer only as a member of its project or as an administrator included; so that a mistake in matching a person elsewhere, or an unverified webhook, answers nothing that was never theirs. In a call made for a person (`ctx.plugins.get(key).for(personId)`), `ctx.console.people`, `teams` and `projects`, and the projects a call reaches, are the person's as a member, `projects:read:all` included. What a plugin another calls as itself writes on the board names no `via`, since it acts for nobody. `ctx.console.people.granted(role)` lists, for a role that follows the reporting line, whoever was granted it besides those who manage someone. `GET /api/v1/plugins/<key>` answers `asks` to an administrator alone, a role the plugin holds as the callee's `defaultRole` standing as granted.
